| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | Whales are really big. | Las ballenas son muy grandes. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | Yes. It names a similarity (“both… live in water”) and a difference (“a whale breathes air while a fish uses gills”), using comparison words. | Sí. Nombra una semejanza (“both… live in water”) y una diferencia (“a whale breathes air while a fish uses gills”), usando palabras de comparación. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | “Are different” doesn't say how. A strong comparison names the difference. | “Are different” no dice cómo. Una buena comparación nombra la diferencia. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › hint | Which choice uses “both” and “but/while”? | ¿Qué opción usa “both” y “but/while”? |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | This describes only whales. A comparison talks about both things. | Esto describe solo a las ballenas. Una comparación habla de las dos cosas. |
| items › #2 › context | Compare rain and snow. | Compara la lluvia y la nieve. |
| items › #2 › prompt | Which sentence compares them best? | ¿Qué oración los compara mejor? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Try “Both ___; however, ___.” / Prueba “Both ___; however, ___.” | Prueba “Both ___; however, ___.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | Rain and snow both fall from clouds; however, rain is liquid and snow is frozen. | La lluvia y la nieve caen de las nubes; sin embargo, la lluvia es líquida y la nieve está congelada. |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | Rain is wet. | La lluvia es mojada. |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | They are not the same thing. | No son lo mismo. |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Yes. It names the similarity (“both fall from clouds”) and the difference (“rain is liquid… snow is frozen”) with “however.” | Sí. Nombra la semejanza (“both fall from clouds”) y la diferencia (“rain is liquid… snow is frozen”) con “however.” |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | This describes only rain. Compare both, and name the difference. | Esto describe solo la lluvia. Compara las dos y nombra la diferencia. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Say what's the same AND what's different. | Di qué es igual Y qué es diferente. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | “Not the same” doesn't say how. Name the difference: liquid vs. frozen. | “Not the same” no dice cómo. Nombra la diferencia: líquida vs. congelada. |
| items › #3 › context | Compare a triangle and a square. | Compara un triángulo y un cuadrado. |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which sentence compares them best? | ¿Qué oración los compara mejor? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Try “A ___ has ___, whereas a ___ has ___.” / Prueba “A ___ has ___, whereas a ___ has ___.” | Prueba “A ___ has ___, whereas a ___ has ___.” |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | A triangle has three sides, whereas a square has four. | Un triángulo tiene tres lados, mientras que un cuadrado tiene cuatro. |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | A square is a shape. | Un cuadrado es una figura. |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | Shapes can be different. | Las figuras pueden ser diferentes. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | Yes. It compares the exact difference (three sides vs. four) using “whereas.” | Sí. Compara la diferencia exacta (tres lados vs. cuatro) usando “whereas.” |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | This describes only the square. Compare both shapes' sides. | Esto describe solo el cuadrado. Compara los lados de las dos figuras. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | How many sides does each shape have? | ¿Cuántos lados tiene cada figura? |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | “Can be different” is vague. Name the exact difference in sides. | “Can be different” es vago. Nombra la diferencia exacta en los lados. |
| items › #4 › context | Compare the sun and the moon. | Compara el sol y la luna. |
| items › #4 › prompt | Which sentence compares them best? | ¿Qué oración los compara mejor? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Name what's the same and the key difference (light). / Nombra lo que es igual y la diferencia clave (la luz). | Nombra lo que es igual y la diferencia clave (la luz). |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | Both the sun and the moon appear in the sky, but the sun makes its own light while the moon reflects it. | Tanto el sol como la luna aparecen en el cielo, pero el sol produce su propia luz mientras que la luna la refleja. |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | The sun is bright. | El sol es brillante. |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | The sun and the moon are in space. | El sol y la luna están en el espacio. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | Yes. It names the similarity (“both… appear in the sky”) and the key difference (makes light vs. reflects light) with “but/while.” | Sí. Nombra la semejanza (“both… appear in the sky”) y la diferencia clave (produce luz vs. la refleja) con “but/while.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | This describes only the sun. Compare both, and name the difference. | Esto describe solo el sol. Compara los dos y nombra la diferencia. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › hint | Which one makes light, and which reflects it? | ¿Cuál produce luz y cuál la refleja? |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | This only names a similarity. A strong comparison also names a difference. | Esto solo nombra una semejanza. Una buena comparación también nombra una diferencia. |

### Say It the Academic Way  `all-academic-6-8-008` — 60 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Say It the Academic Way | Dílo de forma académica |
| studentGoal | Take an everyday statement and say it the academic way — using precise content-area words so your meaning is clear in a class discussion or on a test. | Toma una afirmación cotidiana y díla de forma académica, usando palabras precisas del área de contenido para que tu significado sea claro en una discusión de clase o en un examen. |
| studentDirections | Each item shows a casual way to say something. Choose the response that says the SAME idea in a more academic, precise way. Choose, then select Check. | Cada ejercicio muestra una forma casual de decir algo. Elige la respuesta que dice la MISMA idea de forma más académica y precisa. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Middle-school content classes expect precise academic vocabulary. Each item contrasts a casual statement with an academic restatement; the distractors are either vague or wordy-but-imprecise. Turn on More support for a word bank and a sentence frame. Have students say the academic version aloud and use it in their next written response. | Las clases de contenido de secundaria esperan vocabulario académico preciso. Cada ejercicio contrasta una afirmación casual con una reformulación académica; los distractores son vagos o largos pero imprecisos. Activa Más apoyo para ver un banco de palabras y un marco de oración. Pide a los estudiantes que digan la versión académica en voz alta y la usen en su siguiente respuesta escrita. |
| languageFunction | Restating ideas with precise academic and content-area vocabulary. | Reformular ideas con vocabulario académico y de contenido preciso. |
| items › #1 › context | In science class, a student says: “The water went up into the air and disappeared.” | En la clase de ciencias, un estudiante dice: “The water went up into the air and disappeared.” |
| items › #1 › prompt | Which says the same idea the academic way? | ¿Cuál dice la misma idea de forma académica? |
| items › #1 › support › high | Word bank: evaporate, water vapor, atmosphere. Frame: “The water ___ and rose into the ___ as ___.” | Banco de palabras: evaporate, water vapor, atmosphere. Marco: “The water ___ and rose into the ___ as ___.” |
| items › #1 › support › medium | What is the science word for water turning into a gas? | ¿Cuál es la palabra de ciencia para cuando el agua se convierte en gas? |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | “The water evaporated and rose into the atmosphere as water vapor.” | “The water evaporated and rose into the atmosphere as water vapor.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | “The water just kind of went away up there.” | “The water just kind of went away up there.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | “The water went up really high into the big sky and then it was gone somehow.” | “The water went up really high into the big sky and then it was gone somehow.” |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | Right. “Evaporated,” “atmosphere,” and “water vapor” are the precise science words — clear and correct on a test or in discussion. | Correcto. “Evaporated,” “atmosphere” y “water vapor” son las palabras de ciencia precisas: claras y correctas en un examen o en una discusión. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | This is still casual and vague (“kind of went away”). The academic version names the process: evaporated into water vapor. | Esto sigue siendo casual y vago (“kind of went away”). La versión académica nombra el proceso: evaporated into water vapor. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › hint | Use the science word for turning into gas. | Usa la palabra de ciencia para convertirse en gas. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | Longer isn't more academic — this is wordy and still vague (“gone somehow”). Precise words beat extra words. | Más largo no es más académico: esto es palabrero y aún vago (“gone somehow”). Las palabras precisas superan a las palabras de más. |
| items › #2 › context | In math class, a student describes a graph: “The line goes up as you go to the right.” | En la clase de matemáticas, un estudiante describe una gráfica: “The line goes up as you go to the right.” |
| items › #2 › prompt | Which says it the academic way? | ¿Cuál lo dice de forma académica? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Word bank: increases, positive slope. Frame: “As x increases, y ___, so the line has a ___ slope.” | Banco de palabras: increases, positive slope. Marco: “As x increases, y ___, so the line has a ___ slope.” |
| items › #2 › support › medium | What math word describes a line that rises? | ¿Qué palabra de matemáticas describe una línea que sube? |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | “As x increases, y increases, so the line has a positive slope.” | “As x increases, y increases, so the line has a positive slope.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | “The line is going up and stuff.” | “The line is going up and stuff.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | “The line is happy because it keeps climbing higher and higher.” | “The line is happy because it keeps climbing higher and higher.” |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Right. “Increases” and “positive slope” are the precise math terms that describe the relationship exactly. | Correcto. “Increases” y “positive slope” son los términos matemáticos precisos que describen la relación exactamente. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | “And stuff” is casual filler. The academic version names the variables and the slope. | “And stuff” es relleno casual. La versión académica nombra las variables y la pendiente. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Use “increases” and “positive slope.” | Usa “increases” y “positive slope.” |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | A line doesn't have feelings — this is imprecise. Describe it with variables and slope. | Una línea no tiene sentimientos: esto es impreciso. Descríbela con variables y pendiente. |
| items › #3 › context | In social studies, a student says: “A lot of people moved to the city to find jobs.” | En estudios sociales, un estudiante dice: “A lot of people moved to the city to find jobs.” |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which uses the academic term? | ¿Cuál usa el término académico? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Word bank: migrated, in search of. Frame: “Many people ___ to the city in search of ___.” | Banco de palabras: migrated, in search of. Marco: “Many people ___ to the city in search of ___.” |
| items › #3 › support › medium | What is the social-studies word for people moving to a new place? | ¿Cuál es la palabra de estudios sociales para la gente que se muda a un lugar nuevo? |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | “Many people migrated to the city in search of work.” | “Many people migrated to the city in search of work.” |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | “Tons of people went over there for jobs.” | “Tons of people went over there for jobs.” |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | “People moved to the city because the city had jobs that people wanted to get.” | “People moved to the city because the city had jobs that people wanted to get.” |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | Right. “Migrated” is the precise social-studies term, and “in search of work” states the cause clearly. | Correcto. “Migrated” es el término preciso de estudios sociales, y “in search of work” expresa la causa con claridad. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | “Tons of people… over there” is casual. The academic word is “migrated.” | “Tons of people… over there” es casual. La palabra académica es “migrated.” |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | Use the word “migrated.” | Usa la palabra “migrated.” |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | This repeats “jobs” and “city” and never uses the precise term. Academic writing is precise, not repetitive. | Esto repite “jobs” y “city” y nunca usa el término preciso. La escritura académica es precisa, no repetitiva. |
| items › #4 › context | In an ELAR discussion, a student says: “The character was sad, but at the end he felt better.” | En una discusión de ELAR, un estudiante dice: “The character was sad, but at the end he felt better.” |
| items › #4 › prompt | Which states it more precisely? | ¿Cuál lo expresa con más precisión? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Word bank: mood, shifted, despair, relief. Frame: “The character's mood shifted from ___ to ___.” | Banco de palabras: mood, shifted, despair, relief. Marco: “The character's mood shifted from ___ to ___.” |
| items › #4 › support › medium | What literary word means how a character feels across the text? | ¿Qué palabra literaria significa cómo se siente un personaje a lo largo del texto? |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | “The character's mood shifted from despair to relief by the end of the chapter.” | “The character's mood shifted from despair to relief by the end of the chapter.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | “He was down and then he was okay I guess.” | “He was down and then he was okay I guess.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | “The character felt a lot of different feelings that changed from the start to the end a lot.” | “The character felt a lot of different feelings that changed from the start to the end a lot.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | Right. “Mood shifted,” “despair,” and “relief” are precise literary words that show the change exactly. | Correcto. “Mood shifted,” “despair” y “relief” son palabras literarias precisas que muestran el cambio exactamente. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | “Okay I guess” is casual and uncertain. The academic version names the mood and the shift. | “Okay I guess” es casual e inseguro. La versión académica nombra el estado de ánimo y el cambio. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › hint | Use “mood shifted from ___ to ___.” | Usa “mood shifted from ___ to ___.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | This is long and vague (“a lot of different feelings… a lot”). Name the specific moods instead. | Esto es largo y vago (“a lot of different feelings… a lot”). Nombra los estados de ánimo específicos. |
| items › #5 › context | Looking at a bar graph of monthly rainfall, a student says: “There was more rain in July than the other months.” | Mirando una gráfica de barras de lluvia mensual, un estudiante dice: “There was more rain in July than the other months.” |
| items › #5 › prompt | Which reports the data the academic way? | ¿Cuál reporta los datos de forma académica? |
| items › #5 › support › high | Word bank: data show, greatest value. Frame: “The data show that the ___ value of ___ occurred in ___.” | Banco de palabras: data show, greatest value. Marco: “The data show that the ___ value of ___ occurred in ___.” |
| items › #5 › support › medium | How do you point to evidence from a graph in academic language? | ¿Cómo señalas evidencia de una gráfica en lenguaje académico? |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | “The data show that the greatest amount of rainfall occurred in July.” | “The data show that the greatest amount of rainfall occurred in July.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | “July had like the most rain for sure.” | “July had like the most rain for sure.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | “It rained a whole lot in July and that is what the picture is showing us here.” | “It rained a whole lot in July and that is what the picture is showing us here.” |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | Right. “The data show” and “greatest amount” are the academic phrases for citing evidence from a graph. | Correcto. “The data show” y “greatest amount” son las frases académicas para citar evidencia de una gráfica. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | “Like… for sure” is casual. Academic reporting says “the data show.” | “Like… for sure” es casual. El reporte académico dice “the data show.” |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | Start with “The data show…” | Empieza con “The data show…” |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | “Picture” and “a whole lot” are casual and imprecise. Say “the data show the greatest amount.” | “Picture” y “a whole lot” son casuales e imprecisos. Di “the data show the greatest amount.” |

### Academic Talk Moves (Middle School)  `all-talk-6-8-009` — 60 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Academic Talk Moves (Middle School) | Movimientos de conversación académica (secundaria) |
| studentGoal | Use academic talk moves — add a reason, disagree politely, ask for clarification, build on a peer — to contribute well to a class discussion. | Usa movimientos de conversación académica —agregar una razón, estar en desacuerdo con cortesía, pedir una aclaración, ampliar lo que dice un compañero— para participar bien en una discusión de clase. |
| studentDirections | Read what a classmate said and what you want to do. Choose the response that makes that talk move well. Choose, then select Check. | Lee lo que dijo un compañero y lo que tú quieres hacer. Elige la respuesta que hace bien ese movimiento de conversación. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Discussion is where academic language is rehearsed. Each item targets one talk move (add a reason, respectfully disagree, clarify, build on, paraphrase). Post the frames and have students use the chosen move aloud in the next discussion. No TEKS is forced — ELPS speaking is the on-target standard for these interactions. | La discusión es donde se practica el lenguaje académico. Cada ejercicio trabaja un movimiento (agregar una razón, estar en desacuerdo con respeto, aclarar, ampliar, parafrasear). Publica los marcos y pide que usen el movimiento elegido en voz alta en la siguiente discusión. No se fuerza un TEKS: el estándar ELPS de expresión oral es el adecuado para estas interacciones. |
| languageFunction | Contributing to academic discussion with specific talk moves. | Contribuir a la discusión académica con movimientos específicos. |
| items › #1 › context | In science, Ana says, “Metals get hot quickly.” You agree and want to ADD A REASON. | En ciencias, Ana dice: “Metals get hot quickly.” Estás de acuerdo y quieres AGREGAR UNA RAZÓN. |
| items › #1 › prompt | Which response adds a reason best? | ¿Qué respuesta agrega mejor una razón? |
| items › #1 › support › high | Frame: “I agree, and the reason is that ___.” | Marco: “I agree, and the reason is that ___.” |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Agree AND explain WHY with a science idea. | Está de acuerdo Y explica POR QUÉ con una idea de ciencia. |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | “I agree, and the reason is that metals are good conductors of heat.” | “I agree, and the reason is that metals are good conductors of heat.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | “Yeah, same.” | “Yeah, same.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | “I don't know, maybe.” | “I don't know, maybe.” |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | Right. You agree AND give a reason with a science term (conductors) — that is the talk move. | Correcto. Estás de acuerdo Y das una razón con un término de ciencia (conductors): ese es el movimiento. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | “Same” agrees but gives no reason. Add WHY: “the reason is that metals conduct heat.” | “Same” está de acuerdo pero no da razón. Agrega POR QUÉ: “the reason is that metals conduct heat.” |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › hint | What makes metals heat up fast? | ¿Qué hace que los metales se calienten rápido? |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | This shows doubt, not a reason. The move is to agree and explain why. | Esto muestra duda, no una razón. El movimiento es estar de acuerdo y explicar por qué. |
| items › #2 › context | A classmate says, “All deserts are hot.” You think that's not fully true and want to DISAGREE POLITELY. | Un compañero dice: “All deserts are hot.” Crees que no es del todo cierto y quieres ESTAR EN DESACUERDO CON CORTESÍA. |
| items › #2 › prompt | Which is the best respectful disagreement? | ¿Cuál es el mejor desacuerdo respetuoso? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Frame: “I see your point, but ___, because ___.” | Marco: “I see your point, but ___, because ___.” |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Be respectful AND give a counter-example. | Sé respetuoso Y da un contraejemplo. |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | “I see your point, but some deserts are cold, like the Gobi in winter.” | “I see your point, but some deserts are cold, like the Gobi in winter.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | “No, you're wrong.” | “No, you're wrong.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | “Deserts, deserts, deserts.” | “Deserts, deserts, deserts.” |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Right. You acknowledge their point, then disagree with a specific example — respectful and evidence-based. | Correcto. Reconoces su punto y luego discrepas con un ejemplo específico: respetuoso y con evidencia. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | “You're wrong” is blunt and gives no evidence. Soften it and add an example. | “You're wrong” es brusco y no da evidencia. Suavízalo y agrega un ejemplo. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Start with “I see your point, but…” | Empieza con “I see your point, but…” |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | Repeating the word adds nothing. Disagree politely with a reason or example. | Repetir la palabra no aporta nada. Discrepa con cortesía con una razón o ejemplo. |
| items › #3 › context | The teacher uses the word “erosion” and you are not sure what it means. You want to ASK FOR CLARIFICATION. | El maestro usa la palabra “erosion” y no estás seguro de qué significa. Quieres PEDIR UNA ACLARACIÓN. |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which is the best way to ask? | ¿Cuál es la mejor manera de preguntar? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Frame: “Could you explain what ___ means?” | Marco: “Could you explain what ___ means?” |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Ask a clear, polite question about the word. | Haz una pregunta clara y cortés sobre la palabra. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | “Could you explain what ‘erosion’ means?” | “Could you explain what ‘erosion’ means?” |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | (Say nothing and stay confused.) | (No decir nada y quedarte confundido.) |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | “What?” | “What?” |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | Right. A clear question names the word and asks for its meaning — exactly the clarification move. | Correcto. Una pregunta clara nombra la palabra y pide su significado: justo el movimiento de aclaración. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | Staying confused means you miss the lesson. Asking about a word is a smart academic move. | Quedarte confundido hace que pierdas la lección. Preguntar por una palabra es un movimiento académico inteligente. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | Name the word you don't know. | Nombra la palabra que no conoces. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | “What?” is vague and the teacher won't know what to explain. Name the word: “what does ‘erosion’ mean?” | “What?” es vago y el maestro no sabrá qué explicar. Nombra la palabra: “what does ‘erosion’ mean?” |
| items › #4 › context | In a discussion, Diego says, “Rivers shape the land.” You want to BUILD ON his idea. | En una discusión, Diego dice: “Rivers shape the land.” Quieres AMPLIAR su idea. |
| items › #4 › prompt | Which response builds on Diego's idea? | ¿Qué respuesta amplía la idea de Diego? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Frame: “Building on what Diego said, ___.” | Marco: “Building on what Diego said, ___.” |
| items › #4 › support › medium | Connect to Diego AND add a new detail. | Conéctate con Diego Y agrega un detalle nuevo. |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | “Building on what Diego said, rivers also carry soil that builds new land at the mouth.” | “Building on what Diego said, rivers also carry soil that builds new land at the mouth.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | “I like rivers too.” | “I like rivers too.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | “Let's talk about something else.” | “Let's talk about something else.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | Right. You name Diego's idea and extend it with a new detail — that is building on a peer. | Correcto. Nombras la idea de Diego y la amplías con un detalle nuevo: eso es ampliar lo de un compañero. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | Liking rivers doesn't extend the idea. Add a new detail that connects to what Diego said. | Que te gusten los ríos no amplía la idea. Agrega un detalle nuevo que se conecte con lo que dijo Diego. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › hint | Start with “Building on what Diego said…” | Empieza con “Building on what Diego said…” |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | Changing the topic drops Diego's idea instead of building on it. | Cambiar de tema abandona la idea de Diego en vez de ampliarla. |
| items › #5 › context | Your group wants to make sure everyone understood Lin's point. You want to PARAPHRASE what she said: “The experiment failed because the temperature was too high.” | Tu grupo quiere asegurarse de que todos entendieron el punto de Lin. Quieres PARAFRASEAR lo que dijo: “The experiment failed because the temperature was too high.” |
| items › #5 › prompt | Which response paraphrases Lin's point well? | ¿Qué respuesta parafrasea bien el punto de Lin? |
| items › #5 › support › high | Frame: “So what you're saying is ___.” Say the same idea in your own words. | Marco: “So what you're saying is ___.” Di la misma idea con tus palabras. |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Restate HER idea, don't add a new one. | Reformula SU idea, no agregues una nueva. |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | “So what you're saying is the heat was too high, and that's why it didn't work.” | “So what you're saying is the heat was too high, and that's why it didn't work.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | “So what you're saying is we should use more water.” | “So what you're saying is we should use more water.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | “So what you're saying is experiments are hard.” | “So what you're saying is experiments are hard.” |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | Right. You restate Lin's exact idea — too much heat caused the failure — in your own words. That's a paraphrase. | Correcto. Reformulas la idea exacta de Lin —demasiado calor causó el fallo— con tus palabras. Eso es parafrasear. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | Lin didn't mention water — that adds a new idea. A paraphrase repeats HER point. | Lin no mencionó agua: eso agrega una idea nueva. Una paráfrasis repite SU punto. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | What was Lin's actual reason? | ¿Cuál fue la razón real de Lin? |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | That's too general. Lin named a specific cause — high temperature. Paraphrase that. | Eso es muy general. Lin nombró una causa específica: la temperatura alta. Parafrasea eso. |

### Formal or Informal?  `all-register-9-12-010` — 60 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Formal or Informal? | ¿Formal o informal? |
| studentGoal | Match your register — how formal your language is — to the situation, so you sound appropriate with a teacher, in an essay, or with a friend. | Ajusta tu registro —qué tan formal es tu lenguaje— a la situación, para sonar apropiado con un maestro, en un ensayo o con un amigo. |
| studentDirections | Read each situation. Choose the response with the right register for that audience. Choose, then select Check. | Lee cada situación. Elige la respuesta con el registro correcto para ese público. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Academic settings demand register control: an email to a teacher and a text to a friend are not the same English. Each item pairs a context with an audience; note that informal is sometimes the correct answer (with a friend) — the skill is matching, not always being formal. ELPS speaking-register is the on-target standard. | Los entornos académicos exigen control del registro: un correo a un maestro y un mensaje a un amigo no son el mismo inglés. Cada ejercicio empareja un contexto con un público; nota que a veces lo informal es la respuesta correcta (con un amigo): la destreza es ajustar, no siempre ser formal. El estándar adecuado es el de registro oral del ELPS. |
| languageFunction | Selecting an appropriate formal or informal register for the audience. | Seleccionar un registro formal o informal apropiado para el público. |
| items › #1 › context | You are writing an email to your teacher to ask about a missing grade. | Estás escribiendo un correo a tu maestro para preguntar por una calificación faltante. |
| items › #1 › prompt | Which opening has the right register? | ¿Qué inicio tiene el registro correcto? |
| items › #1 › support › high | An email to a teacher is FORMAL. Use a greeting and a full, polite sentence. | Un correo a un maestro es FORMAL. Usa un saludo y una oración completa y cortés. |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Would you write to a teacher the way you text a friend? | ¿Le escribirías a un maestro como le mandas mensaje a un amigo? |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | “Dear Mr. Lopez, I noticed a grade is missing for the lab report. Could you please check it?” | “Dear Mr. Lopez, I noticed a grade is missing for the lab report. Could you please check it?” |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | “yo where my grade at” | “yo where my grade at” |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | “GRADE MISSING. FIX NOW.” | “GRADE MISSING. FIX NOW.” |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | Right. A greeting and a polite, complete request is the formal register an email to a teacher needs. | Correcto. Un saludo y una petición cortés y completa es el registro formal que necesita un correo a un maestro. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | That's texting register — too casual for a teacher email. Use a greeting and a full sentence. | Ese es el registro de los mensajes: demasiado casual para un correo a un maestro. Usa un saludo y una oración completa. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › hint | Email a teacher = formal. | Correo a un maestro = formal. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | All caps and commands sound rude. A formal, polite request works better. | Las mayúsculas y las órdenes suenan groseras. Una petición formal y cortés funciona mejor. |
| items › #2 › context | You are giving a formal presentation to the class about your research. | Estás dando una presentación formal a la clase sobre tu investigación. |
| items › #2 › prompt | Which sentence fits a formal presentation? | ¿Qué oración encaja en una presentación formal? |
| items › #2 › support › high | A presentation is formal. State your point clearly and completely. | Una presentación es formal. Expón tu punto con claridad y de forma completa. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Avoid slang and filler in a presentation. | Evita la jerga y el relleno en una presentación. |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | “Today I will present three causes of coastal erosion.” | “Today I will present three causes of coastal erosion.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | “Okay so, like, erosion and stuff, here we go.” | “Okay so, like, erosion and stuff, here we go.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | “idk just look at my slides” | “idk just look at my slides” |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Right. It clearly states the topic and plan in a formal register suited to a presentation. | Correcto. Expone el tema y el plan con claridad en un registro formal adecuado para una presentación. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | “Like… and stuff” is casual filler. A presentation needs a clear, formal statement. | “Like… and stuff” es relleno casual. Una presentación necesita una afirmación clara y formal. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | State your topic clearly. | Expón tu tema con claridad. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | “idk” is texting slang, far too informal to present with. State your point. | “idk” es jerga de mensajes, demasiado informal para presentar. Expón tu punto. |
| items › #3 › context | You are texting a close friend to ask if they want to study together after school. | Le mandas un mensaje a un amigo cercano para preguntarle si quiere estudiar juntos después de clases. |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which register fits texting a friend? | ¿Qué registro encaja al escribirle a un amigo? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Texting a friend can be INFORMAL — relaxed and friendly is fine here. | Escribirle a un amigo puede ser INFORMAL: relajado y amistoso está bien aquí. |
| items › #3 › support › medium | You don't need a formal greeting with a close friend. | No necesitas un saludo formal con un amigo cercano. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | “hey wanna study together after school?” | “hey wanna study together after school?” |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | “Dear Sir or Madam, I hereby request your presence for study.” | “Dear Sir or Madam, I hereby request your presence for study.” |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | “TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN: STUDY SESSION.” | “TO WHOM IT MAY CONCERN: STUDY SESSION.” |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | Right. With a close friend, an informal, relaxed message is appropriate — register means matching the audience, not always being formal. | Correcto. Con un amigo cercano, un mensaje informal y relajado es apropiado: el registro es ajustarse al público, no siempre ser formal. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | “Dear Sir or Madam… hereby” is far too formal for a friend. Informal fits here. | “Dear Sir or Madam… hereby” es demasiado formal para un amigo. Aquí encaja lo informal. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | Who are you writing to — a friend or an official? | ¿A quién le escribes: un amigo o una autoridad? |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | A business opening to a friend is overly formal and odd. Keep it casual with a friend. | Un encabezado formal de negocios con un amigo es exagerado y raro. Con un amigo mantenlo casual. |
| items › #4 › context | You want to ask the school principal for permission to start a new club. | Quieres pedir permiso al director de la escuela para iniciar un club nuevo. |
| items › #4 › prompt | Which request has the right register? | ¿Qué petición tiene el registro correcto? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Speaking to a principal is FORMAL. Be polite, clear, and complete. | Hablar con un director es FORMAL. Sé cortés, claro y completo. |
| items › #4 › support › medium | Use a formal greeting and a clear request. | Usa un saludo formal y una petición clara. |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | “Good morning, Principal Reyes. I would like to request permission to start a coding club. May I explain my plan?” | “Good morning, Principal Reyes. I would like to request permission to start a coding club. May I explain my plan?” |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | “Yo, I'm making a club, cool?” | “Yo, I'm making a club, cool?” |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | “Club. Me. Yes or no.” | “Club. Me. Yes or no.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | Right. A formal greeting and a clear, polite request is the register for a principal. | Correcto. Un saludo formal y una petición clara y cortés es el registro para un director. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | “Yo… cool?” is friend register, too casual for a principal. Be formal and clear. | “Yo… cool?” es registro de amigos, demasiado casual para un director. Sé formal y claro. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › hint | How formal is a principal? | ¿Qué tan formal es un director? |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | Clipped commands sound rude to an authority. Use a complete, polite request. | Las órdenes cortadas suenan groseras con una autoridad. Usa una petición completa y cortés. |
| items › #5 › context | You are writing a sentence for a formal class essay about a novel. | Estás escribiendo una oración para un ensayo formal de clase sobre una novela. |
| items › #5 › prompt | Which sentence fits a formal essay? | ¿Qué oración encaja en un ensayo formal? |
| items › #5 › support › high | Essays are formal: no slang, no “you,” complete academic sentences. | Los ensayos son formales: sin jerga, sin “you,” oraciones académicas completas. |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Avoid “a lot” and texting words in an essay. | Evita “a lot” y palabras de mensajes en un ensayo. |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | “The author uses the storm to reflect the character's inner conflict.” | “The author uses the storm to reflect the character's inner conflict.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | “The book was lowkey really good ngl.” | “The book was lowkey really good ngl.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | “You'll cry a lot when you read it trust me.” | “You'll cry a lot when you read it trust me.” |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | Right. It uses formal, analytical language with no slang — the register an essay requires. | Correcto. Usa lenguaje formal y analítico sin jerga: el registro que exige un ensayo. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | “lowkey… ngl” is social-media slang, not essay register. Write a formal, analytical sentence. | “lowkey… ngl” es jerga de redes sociales, no registro de ensayo. Escribe una oración formal y analítica. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | Would “ngl” appear in a formal essay? | ¿Aparecería “ngl” en un ensayo formal? |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | “Trust me” and direct “you” are too casual for a formal essay. Keep it analytical. | “Trust me” y el “you” directo son demasiado casuales para un ensayo formal. Mantenlo analítico. |

### Plan Your Presentation  `all-present-3-5-011` — 60 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Plan Your Presentation | Planea tu presentación |
| studentGoal | Plan and give a short, organized spoken presentation: a clear opening, ordered points, a strong close, and good delivery. | Planea y da una presentación oral corta y organizada: una apertura clara, puntos ordenados, un cierre fuerte y una buena entrega. |
| studentDirections | You are getting ready to present. Choose the best choice for each part of a clear presentation. Choose, then select Check. | Te estás preparando para presentar. Elige la mejor opción para cada parte de una presentación clara. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Builds toward the grade-5 speaking expectation: an organized presentation with eye contact, appropriate rate and volume, and conventions of language. Each item isolates one feature (opening, order, delivery, visuals, closing). Pair with an actual 1-minute presentation so students apply the choices. | Avanza hacia la expectativa de expresión oral de 5.º grado: una presentación organizada con contacto visual, ritmo y volumen apropiados, y convenciones del lenguaje. Cada ejercicio aísla una característica (apertura, orden, entrega, apoyos visuales, cierre). Combínalo con una presentación real de 1 minuto para que apliquen las opciones. |
| languageFunction | Organizing and delivering an oral presentation. | Organizar y dar una presentación oral. |
| items › #1 › context | You are presenting about your favorite animal, the dolphin. | Vas a presentar sobre tu animal favorito, el delfín. |
| items › #1 › prompt | What is the best way to OPEN? | ¿Cuál es la mejor manera de EMPEZAR? |
| items › #1 › support › high | A good opening tells the audience your topic clearly. | Una buena apertura le dice al público tu tema con claridad. |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Say what your presentation is about. | Di de qué trata tu presentación. |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | “Today I will tell you three amazing facts about dolphins.” | “Today I will tell you three amazing facts about dolphins.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | “Um… I don't know how to start.” | “Um… I don't know how to start.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | (Begin talking about sharks instead.) | (Empezar a hablar de tiburones en su lugar.) |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | Right. A clear opening names your topic and tells the audience what's coming. | Correcto. Una apertura clara nombra tu tema y le dice al público qué viene. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | Starting with “I don't know” loses your audience. Open by naming your topic. | Empezar con “I don't know” pierde a tu público. Abre nombrando tu tema. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › hint | Tell them your topic. | Diles tu tema. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | Your topic is dolphins, not sharks. Open on topic so the audience follows. | Tu tema son los delfines, no los tiburones. Abre con tu tema para que el público te siga. |
| items › #2 › context | You have three facts to share about dolphins. | Tienes tres hechos para compartir sobre los delfines. |
| items › #2 › prompt | What is the best way to ORDER your points? | ¿Cuál es la mejor manera de ORDENAR tus puntos? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Use order words: first, next, finally. | Usa palabras de orden: first, next, finally. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Group your facts so they are easy to follow. | Agrupa tus hechos para que sean fáciles de seguir. |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | “First, dolphins are mammals. Next, they use sound to find food. Finally, they live in groups.” | “First, dolphins are mammals. Next, they use sound to find food. Finally, they live in groups.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | Say all three facts at the same time in one long rush. | Decir los tres hechos al mismo tiempo en una sola carrera larga. |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | Say only one fact and repeat it three times. | Decir solo un hecho y repetirlo tres veces. |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Right. Order words (first, next, finally) make your three points easy to follow. | Correcto. Las palabras de orden (first, next, finally) hacen que tus tres puntos sean fáciles de seguir. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | Rushing everything at once is confusing. Use first, next, finally. | Decir todo de golpe confunde. Usa first, next, finally. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | What words signal order? | ¿Qué palabras señalan el orden? |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | Repeating one fact leaves out the other two. Share all three in order. | Repetir un hecho deja fuera los otros dos. Comparte los tres en orden. |
| items › #3 › context | You are standing in front of the class, about to speak. | Estás parado frente a la clase, a punto de hablar. |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which delivery is best? | ¿Cuál es la mejor entrega? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Look at the audience and speak clearly, not too fast, not too quiet. | Mira al público y habla con claridad, ni muy rápido ni muy bajo. |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Think about eye contact, volume, and speed. | Piensa en el contacto visual, el volumen y la velocidad. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | Look at the audience and speak clearly at a steady pace. | Mirar al público y hablar con claridad a un ritmo constante. |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | Look at the floor and whisper very fast. | Mirar al piso y susurrar muy rápido. |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | Turn your back and read every word off the wall. | Dar la espalda y leer cada palabra de la pared. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | Right. Eye contact, a clear voice, and a steady pace help the audience understand you. | Correcto. El contacto visual, una voz clara y un ritmo constante ayudan al público a entenderte. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | Whispering fast at the floor makes you hard to hear and follow. Look up and speak clearly. | Susurrar rápido al piso te hace difícil de oír y seguir. Levanta la vista y habla con claridad. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | Where should your eyes and voice go? | ¿Hacia dónde deben ir tus ojos y tu voz? |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | Facing away from the audience hides your voice and face. Face them and speak clearly. | Dar la espalda al público esconde tu voz y tu cara. Míralos y habla con claridad. |
| items › #4 › context | You made a poster with a photo of a dolphin for your talk. | Hiciste un cartel con una foto de un delfín para tu charla. |
| items › #4 › prompt | How should you use the visual? | ¿Cómo debes usar el apoyo visual? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Show the visual and point to it while you explain the matching fact. | Muestra el apoyo visual y señálalo mientras explicas el hecho que corresponde. |
| items › #4 › support › medium | A visual should help your words, not replace them. | Un apoyo visual debe ayudar a tus palabras, no reemplazarlas. |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | Hold it up and point to the photo as you explain how dolphins swim. | Levantarlo y señalar la foto mientras explicas cómo nadan los delfines. |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | Hide it so no one sees it. | Esconderlo para que nadie lo vea. |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | Hold it in front of your face the whole time. | Sostenerlo frente a tu cara todo el tiempo. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | Right. Showing and pointing to the visual helps the audience connect your words to the picture. | Correcto. Mostrar y señalar el apoyo visual ayuda al público a conectar tus palabras con la imagen. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | Hiding your poster wastes it. Show it and point to it as you explain. | Esconder tu cartel lo desperdicia. Muéstralo y señálalo mientras explicas. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › hint | What is a poster for? | ¿Para qué sirve un cartel? |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | Covering your face blocks your voice and eye contact. Hold it to the side and point. | Taparte la cara bloquea tu voz y tu contacto visual. Sostenlo a un lado y señala. |
| items › #5 › context | You have shared your three facts and are ready to finish. | Ya compartiste tus tres hechos y estás listo para terminar. |
| items › #5 › prompt | What is the best way to CLOSE? | ¿Cuál es la mejor manera de CERRAR? |
| items › #5 › support › high | A good closing wraps up your topic and thanks the audience. | Un buen cierre resume tu tema y agradece al público. |
| items › #5 › support › medium | End on purpose — don't just stop. | Termina a propósito: no te detengas sin más. |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | “Now you know three amazing facts about dolphins. Thank you for listening.” | “Now you know three amazing facts about dolphins. Thank you for listening.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | Stop talking suddenly and walk away. | Dejar de hablar de repente e irte. |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | “Okay, bye, I guess that's it, whatever.” | “Okay, bye, I guess that's it, whatever.” |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | Right. A clear closing sums up your topic and thanks the audience — a strong finish. | Correcto. Un cierre claro resume tu tema y agradece al público: un final fuerte. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | Stopping suddenly leaves the audience unsure you're done. Wrap up and thank them. | Detenerte de repente deja al público sin saber si terminaste. Resume y agradéceles. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | How do you signal you're finished? | ¿Cómo señalas que terminaste? |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | “Whatever” sounds like you don't care. End with a clear summary and a thank-you. | “Whatever” suena a que no te importa. Termina con un resumen claro y un agradecimiento. |

### Tell About the Picture  `all-describe-k-2-013` — 65 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Tell About the Picture | Describe la imagen |
| studentGoal | Look at a picture and choose the sentence that tells the most about it: what it is, plus its color, size, or what it does. | Mira una imagen y elige la oración que dice más sobre ella: qué es, y también su color, su tamaño o lo que hace. |
| studentDirections | Look at the picture. Listen to the three sentences. Which one tells the MOST? Choose, then select Check. Say the sentence out loud! | Mira la imagen. Escucha las tres oraciones. ¿Cuál dice MÁS? Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. ¡Di la oración en voz alta! |
| teacherDirections | Read each sentence aloud and point to the picture as you read. The strong sentence always names the thing and adds one or two describing words (color, size, what it does). After each item, have students say the strong sentence chorally, then make their own with the frame "The ___ is ___ and ___." Turn on More support to show the frame in Spanish. | Lee cada oración en voz alta y señala la imagen mientras lees. La oración fuerte siempre nombra la cosa y agrega una o dos palabras que la describen (color, tamaño, lo que hace). Después de cada ítem, pide que digan la oración fuerte en coro y luego que formen la suya con el marco "The ___ is ___ and ___." Activa Más apoyo para ver el marco en español. |
| languageFunction | Describing a picture with specific detail (naming plus describing words). | Describir una imagen con detalles específicos (nombrar y usar palabras que describen). |
| items › #1 › prompt | Which sentence tells the MOST about the picture? | ¿Qué oración dice MÁS sobre la imagen? |
| items › #1 › support › high | Frame: "The ___ is ___ and ___." / "El ___ es ___ y ___." big = grande, gray = gris. | Marco: "The ___ is ___ and ___." = "El ___ es ___ y ___." big = grande, gray = gris. |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Say what it is. Then tell its size or color. | Di qué es. Luego di su tamaño o su color. |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | It is big. | It is big. |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | The elephant is big and gray. | The elephant is big and gray. |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | I like it. | I like it. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | This is true, but "it" does not say what the animal is. Name it: "The elephant is…" | Es cierto, pero "it" no dice qué animal es. Nómbralo: "The elephant is…" |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › hint | Say the animal's name and its color. | Di el nombre del animal y su color. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | Yes! This sentence names the animal (elephant) and tells two things about it: big and gray. | ¡Sí! Esta oración nombra al animal (elephant = elefante) y dice dos cosas sobre él: big (grande) y gray (gris). |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | This tells how you feel, not what the picture shows. Tell what you see. | Esto dice lo que sientes, no lo que muestra la imagen. Di lo que ves. |
| items › #2 › prompt | Which sentence tells the MOST about the picture? | ¿Qué oración dice MÁS sobre la imagen? |
| items › #2 › support › high | yellow = amarillo. takes kids to school = lleva a los niños a la escuela. | yellow = amarillo. takes kids to school = lleva a los niños a la escuela. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Tell its color AND what it does. | Di su color Y lo que hace. |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | The yellow bus takes kids to school. | The yellow bus takes kids to school. |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | Bus. | Bus. |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | It goes. | It goes. |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Yes! You named the bus, told its color (yellow), and told what it does (takes kids to school). | ¡Sí! Nombraste el autobús, dijiste su color (yellow = amarillo) y lo que hace (lleva a los niños a la escuela). |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | One word names it, but it does not tell anything about it. Add a color or what it does. | Una sola palabra lo nombra, pero no dice nada sobre él. Agrega un color o lo que hace. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | What color is the bus? | ¿De qué color es el autobús? |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | "It goes" does not say what it is or where it goes. Name the bus and tell more. | "It goes" no dice qué es ni adónde va. Nombra el autobús y di más. |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which sentence tells the MOST about the picture? | ¿Qué oración dice MÁS sobre la imagen? |
| items › #3 › support › high | orange = anaranjado, black stripes = rayas negras. | orange = anaranjado, black stripes = rayas negras. |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Look at its colors. What is on its body? | Mira sus colores. ¿Qué tiene en el cuerpo? |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | It is an animal. | It is an animal. |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | Look! | Look! |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | The tiger is orange with black stripes. | The tiger is orange with black stripes. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | True, but many animals are animals! Say which animal and what it looks like. | Es cierto, ¡pero hay muchos animales! Di qué animal es y cómo se ve. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › hint | Name the animal and its colors. | Nombra al animal y sus colores. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | "Look!" asks someone to look, but it does not describe the picture. | "Look!" le pide a alguien que mire, pero no describe la imagen. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | Yes! You named the tiger and told its colors and its stripes. That is a lot of detail. | ¡Sí! Nombraste al tigre y dijiste sus colores y sus rayas. Eso es mucho detalle. |
| items › #4 › prompt | Which sentence tells the MOST about the picture? | ¿Qué oración dice MÁS sobre la imagen? |
| items › #4 › support › high | slide = resbaladilla, swings = columpios. | slide = resbaladilla, swings = columpios. |
| items › #4 › support › medium | What things do you see at the playground? | ¿Qué cosas ves en el patio de juegos? |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | It is fun. | It is fun. |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | The playground has a slide and swings. | The playground has a slide and swings. |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | Outside. | Outside. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | This tells your opinion, but not what is in the picture. Tell what you see. | Esto dice tu opinión, pero no lo que hay en la imagen. Di lo que ves. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › hint | Name two things you can play on. | Nombra dos cosas en las que puedes jugar. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | Yes! You named the place and two things in it: a slide and swings. | ¡Sí! Nombraste el lugar y dos cosas que hay ahí: una resbaladilla (slide) y columpios (swings). |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | "Outside" tells where, but not what the playground has. Say a whole sentence. | "Outside" dice dónde, pero no lo que tiene el patio de juegos. Di una oración completa. |
| items › #5 › prompt | Which sentence tells the MOST about the picture? | ¿Qué oración dice MÁS sobre la imagen? |
| items › #5 › support › high | green = verde, grows in a pot = crece en una maceta. Cactus = cactus (¡casi igual!). | green = verde, grows in a pot = crece en una maceta. Cactus = cactus (¡casi igual!). |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Tell its color and where it is growing. | Di su color y dónde está creciendo. |

| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | It is a plant. | It is a plant. |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | The green cactus grows in a pot. | The green cactus grows in a pot. |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | "Ouch!" is what you might say if you touch it, but it does not describe the picture. | "Ouch!" es lo que dirías si lo tocas, pero no describe la imagen. |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › hint | Name the plant and its color. | Nombra la planta y su color. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | True, but which plant? Say its name and tell more about it. | Es cierto, pero ¿qué planta? Di su nombre y algo más sobre ella. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | Yes! You named the cactus, its color (green), and where it grows (in a pot). | ¡Sí! Nombraste el cactus, su color (green = verde) y dónde crece (en una maceta). |
| items › #6 › prompt | Which sentence tells the MOST about the picture? | ¿Qué oración dice MÁS sobre la imagen? |
| items › #6 › support › high | long = largo, yellow = amarillo. | long = largo, yellow = amarillo. |
| items › #6 › support › medium | Tell its shape and its color. | Di su forma y su color. |
| items › #6 › choices › #1 › label | The banana is long and yellow. | The banana is long and yellow. |
| items › #6 › choices › #2 › label | Yum. | Yum. |
| items › #6 › choices › #3 › label | It is food. | It is food. |
| items › #6 › feedback › a › why | Yes! You named the banana and told its shape (long) and color (yellow). | ¡Sí! Nombraste el plátano y dijiste su forma (long = largo) y su color (yellow = amarillo). |
| items › #6 › feedback › b › why | "Yum" tells that you like it, but not what it looks like. | "Yum" dice que te gusta, pero no cómo se ve. |
