Un saludo formal con el nombre de la maestra, seguido de una oración clara sobre lo que pasó, establece un registro respetuoso. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | This sounds like an order. “Now” makes the message demanding, and it never explains why you need the homework. | Esto suena como una orden. “Now” hace que el mensaje sea exigente, y nunca explica por qué necesitas la tarea. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › hint | Explain your situation before you ask. | Explica tu situación antes de pedir algo. |
| items › #2 › context | Your email needs a subject line so Ms. Garza knows what it is about before she opens it. | Tu correo necesita un asunto para que Ms. Garza sepa de qué trata antes de abrirlo. |
| items › #2 › prompt | Which subject line is clear and appropriate? | ¿Qué asunto es claro y apropiado? |
| items › #2 › support › high | A subject line names the topic in a few words: what + when. / El asunto nombra el tema en pocas palabras: qué + cuándo. | El asunto nombra el tema en pocas palabras: qué + cuándo. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Which one tells the teacher exactly what the email is about? | ¿Cuál le dice a la maestra exactamente de qué trata el correo? |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | “Missing homework from Monday, 3rd period” | “Missing homework from Monday, 3rd period” |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | “!!!!” | “!!!!” |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | “u there?” | “u there?” |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Right. It names the topic (missing homework), the day, and the class period, so the teacher knows what you need before opening it. | Correcto. Nombra el tema (tarea que falta), el día y el periodo de clase, así la maestra sabe qué necesitas antes de abrirlo. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | Punctuation alone gives no information and can seem alarming. A subject line should name the topic. | Los signos solos no dan información y pueden parecer alarmantes. El asunto debe nombrar el tema. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Name the topic and the day. | Nombra el tema y el día. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | “u there?” is a text to a friend. It does not say what the email is about, and “u” is not used in school email. | “u there?” es un mensaje para un amigo. No dice de qué trata el correo, y “u” no se usa en el correo escolar. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › hint | Which choice names the topic? | ¿Qué opción nombra el tema? |
| items › #3 › context | To a friend you would write: “can u send it.” Now write the request to Ms. Garza. | A un amigo le escribirías: “can u send it.” Ahora escribe la petición para Ms. Garza. |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which request is polite and specific? | ¿Qué petición es cortés y específica? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Frame: “Could you please ___?” / “¿Podría por favor ___?” | Marco: “Could you please ___?” (= “¿Podría por favor ___?”). |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Polite requests often start with “Could you please…” | Las peticiones corteses suelen empezar con “Could you please…” |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | “Send it.” | “Send it.” |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | “can u send it lol” | “can u send it lol” |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | “Could you please tell me where I can find Monday's assignment?” | “Could you please tell me where I can find Monday's assignment?” |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | This is a command with no “please,” and “it” is unclear — the teacher has to guess what “it” means. | Esto es una orden sin “please”, y “it” no es claro: la maestra tiene que adivinar a qué se refiere. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › hint | Add “Could you please…” and name the assignment. | Agrega “Could you please…” y nombra la tarea. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | “u” and “lol” belong in texts to friends. The request is also vague about what “it” is. | “u” y “lol” son para mensajes a amigos. La petición también es vaga sobre qué es “it”. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | Use full words and say exactly what you need. | Usa palabras completas y di exactamente qué necesitas. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | Right. “Could you please” is polite, and “Monday's assignment” says exactly what you need. | Correcto. “Could you please” es cortés, y “Monday's assignment” dice exactamente lo que necesitas. |
| items › #4 › context | To a friend you would write: “i was sick so idk what we did.” Now explain this to Ms. Garza. | A un amigo le escribirías: “i was sick so idk what we did.” Ahora explícaselo a Ms. Garza. |
| items › #4 › prompt | Which sentence explains the situation in the right tone? | ¿Qué oración explica la situación con el tono adecuado? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Frame: “I was ___ on ___, so I am not sure ___.” Replace “idk” with “I am not sure.” | Marco: “I was ___ on ___, so I am not sure ___.” Cambia “idk” por “I am not sure” (= “no estoy seguro”). |
| items › #4 › support › medium | Write out the full words, and keep the tone calm and respectful. | Escribe las palabras completas y mantén un tono tranquilo y respetuoso. |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | “I was sick and idk what happened lol” | “I was sick and idk what happened lol” |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | “I was sick on Monday, so I am not sure what we covered in class.” | “I was sick on Monday, so I am not sure what we covered in class.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | “Being sick is not my fault, so you need to tell me what I missed.” | “Being sick is not my fault, so you need to tell me what I missed.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | “idk” and “lol” are texting abbreviations. In an email to a teacher, write the full words: “I am not sure.” | “idk” y “lol” son abreviaturas de mensajes. En un correo a una maestra, escribe las palabras completas: “I am not sure.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › hint | Replace the abbreviations with full words. | Cambia las abreviaturas por palabras completas. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | Right. It gives the reason and the day, uses complete words, and sounds calm — exactly the tone a teacher expects. | Correcto. Da la razón y el día, usa palabras completas y suena tranquilo: justo el tono que espera una maestra. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | The words are formal, but the tone is defensive and demanding (“you need to tell me”). Register includes tone, not just spelling. | Las palabras son formales, pero el tono es defensivo y exigente (“you need to tell me”). El registro incluye el tono, no solo la ortografía. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › hint | Which choice sounds calm and respectful? | ¿Qué opción suena tranquila y respetuosa? |
| items › #5 › context | To a friend you would end with: “thx bye.” Now close your email to Ms. Garza. | A un amigo le terminarías con: “thx bye.” Ahora cierra tu correo a Ms. Garza. |
| items › #5 › prompt | Which closing fits an email to a teacher? | ¿Qué despedida es adecuada para un correo a una maestra? |
| items › #5 › support › high | Frame: “Thank you for ___. Sincerely, [your full name]” / “Sincerely” = “Atentamente”. | Marco: “Thank you for ___. Sincerely, [tu nombre completo]” (“Sincerely” = “Atentamente”). |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Thank the teacher and sign with your full name. | Dale las gracias a la maestra y firma con tu nombre completo. |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | “Thank you for your help. Sincerely, Daniel Ortiz” | “Thank you for your help. Sincerely, Daniel Ortiz” |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | “thx bye” | “thx bye” |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | “That's all. Reply ASAP.” | “That's all. Reply ASAP.” |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | Right. A thank-you, a formal closing (“Sincerely”), and your full name tell the teacher who wrote and end politely. | Correcto. Un agradecimiento, una despedida formal (“Sincerely”) y tu nombre completo le dicen a la maestra quién escribió y cierran con cortesía. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | “thx bye” is a text abbreviation, and without your name the teacher may not know who sent the email. | “thx bye” es una abreviatura de mensaje, y sin tu nombre la maestra quizá no sepa quién envió el correo. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | Write “Thank you” and sign your name. | Escribe “Thank you” y firma con tu nombre. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | “Reply ASAP” pressures the teacher, and there is no thank-you or name. The closing should be polite. | “Reply ASAP” presiona a la maestra, y no hay agradecimiento ni nombre. La despedida debe ser cortés. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › hint | End with thanks, not a demand. | Termina con un agradecimiento, no con una exigencia. |

### Justify Your Thinking  `all-justify-6-8-024` — 65 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Justify Your Thinking | Justifica tu razonamiento |
| studentGoal | Justify an answer or evaluate a classmate's claim with a clear reason and evidence, using academic sentence frames. | Justifica una respuesta o evalúa la afirmación de un compañero con una razón clara y evidencia, usando marcos de oración académicos. |
| studentDirections | Your teacher asks you to justify an answer or evaluate a claim. Choose the response that gives a correct claim AND a reason or evidence. Choose, then select Check. | Tu maestro te pide justificar una respuesta o evaluar una afirmación. Elige la respuesta que da una afirmación correcta Y una razón o evidencia. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | “Justify” and “evaluate” are task words students meet in every content area. Each item has one response with a claim plus specific evidence, and distractors that give no reason, a circular reason, or a flawed reason. Use the CLEAR overlay: name the claim, then point to the evidence. Post the frames and have partners say each correct response aloud before writing their own for a new problem. | “Justificar” y “evaluar” son palabras de instrucción que los estudiantes encuentran en todas las materias. Cada ejercicio tiene una respuesta con afirmación y evidencia específica, y distractores sin razón, con razón circular o con razonamiento erróneo. Usa el apoyo CLEAR: nombra la afirmación y luego señala la evidencia. Publica los marcos y pide a las parejas que digan cada respuesta correcta en voz alta antes de escribir la suya para un problema nuevo. |
| languageFunction | Justifying and evaluating claims orally with reasons and evidence. | Justificar y evaluar afirmaciones oralmente con razones y evidencia. |
| items › #1 › context | Math: Your teacher asks, “Is 3/4 greater than 2/3? Justify your answer.” | Matemáticas: Tu maestro pregunta: “Is 3/4 greater than 2/3? Justify your answer.” |
| items › #1 › prompt | Which response justifies the answer best? | ¿Qué respuesta justifica mejor la respuesta? |
| items › #1 › support › high | Frames: “I know ___ because ___.” / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” | Marcos: “I know ___ because ___.” (= “Sé que ___ porque ___”) / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” |
| items › #1 › support › medium | A justification shows HOW you know — for example, with common denominators. | Una justificación muestra CÓMO lo sabes; por ejemplo, con denominadores comunes. |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | “Yes, 3/4 is bigger. I just know it.” | “Yes, 3/4 is bigger. I just know it.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | “Yes. 3/4 = 9/12 and 2/3 = 8/12, and 9/12 is greater than 8/12, so 3/4 is greater.” | “Yes. 3/4 = 9/12 and 2/3 = 8/12, and 9/12 is greater than 8/12, so 3/4 is greater.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | “Yes, because 4 is bigger than 3.” | “Yes, because 4 is bigger than 3.” |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | The claim is correct, but “I just know it” gives no reason. Justifying means showing how you know. | La afirmación es correcta, pero “I just know it” no da ninguna razón. Justificar significa mostrar cómo lo sabes. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › hint | Which choice shows the math? | ¿Qué opción muestra las matemáticas? |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | Right. Rewriting both fractions with the denominator 12 is evidence anyone can check, and the conclusion follows from it. | Correcto. Escribir ambas fracciones con denominador 12 es evidencia que cualquiera puede comprobar, y la conclusión se deriva de ella. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | The answer is right, but the reason is wrong: a bigger denominator means SMALLER pieces, so comparing 4 and 3 does not prove anything here. | La respuesta es correcta, pero la razón no: un denominador mayor significa partes MÁS PEQUEÑAS, así que comparar 4 y 3 no prueba nada aquí. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › hint | Try a reason that compares equal-sized pieces. | Busca una razón que compare partes del mismo tamaño. |
| items › #2 › context | Science: “Is an ice cube melting a physical change or a chemical change? Justify.” | Ciencias: “Is an ice cube melting a physical change or a chemical change? Justify.” |
| items › #2 › prompt | Which response justifies the answer best? | ¿Qué respuesta justifica mejor la respuesta? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Frames: “I know ___ because ___.” / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” | Marcos: “I know ___ because ___.” (= “Sé que ___ porque ___”) / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Ask: does a NEW substance form? | Pregúntate: ¿se forma una sustancia NUEVA? |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | “It is a physical change because the water changes from solid to liquid, but it is still water — no new substance forms.” | “It is a physical change because the water changes from solid to liquid, but it is still water — no new substance forms.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | “It is a physical change because my partner said so.” | “It is a physical change because my partner said so.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | “It is a chemical change because it looks different.” | “It is a chemical change because it looks different.” |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Right. The claim (physical change) is supported by the key evidence: the substance is still water, so nothing new formed. | Correcto. La afirmación (cambio físico) se apoya en la evidencia clave: la sustancia sigue siendo agua, así que no se formó nada nuevo. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | A classmate's opinion is not scientific evidence. Explain what happens to the substance itself. | La opinión de un compañero no es evidencia científica. Explica qué le pasa a la sustancia misma. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Use evidence about the water, not about a person. | Usa evidencia sobre el agua, no sobre una persona. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | Looking different is not enough — melted ice looks different but is still water. A chemical change makes a new substance. | Verse diferente no es suficiente: el hielo derretido se ve diferente pero sigue siendo agua. Un cambio químico forma una sustancia nueva. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › hint | Did a new substance form? | ¿Se formó una sustancia nueva? |
| items › #3 › context | Science: A classmate claims, “Heavier objects always fall faster, so on the Moon a hammer will land before a feather.” | Ciencias: Un compañero afirma: “Heavier objects always fall faster, so on the Moon a hammer will land before a feather.” |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which response evaluates the claim with a reason? | ¿Qué respuesta evalúa la afirmación con una razón? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Frames: “I know ___ because ___.” / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” | Marcos: “I know ___ because ___.” (= “Sé que ___ porque ___”) / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” |
| items › #3 › support › medium | The Moon has no air. Without air resistance, objects fall at the same rate. | La Luna no tiene aire. Sin resistencia del aire, los objetos caen a la misma velocidad. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | “That's a bad idea.” | “That's a bad idea.” |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | “I'm not sure, maybe.” | “I'm not sure, maybe.” |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | “I disagree, because the Moon has no air resistance, so the hammer and the feather fall at the same rate. Astronauts tested this on Apollo 15.” | “I disagree, because the Moon has no air resistance, so the hammer and the feather fall at the same rate. Astronauts tested this on Apollo 15.” |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | This judges the idea but gives no reason. Evaluating means explaining WHY a claim is or isn't supported. | Esto juzga la idea pero no da ninguna razón. Evaluar significa explicar POR QUÉ una afirmación tiene o no tiene apoyo. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › hint | Use “I disagree because ___.” | Usa “I disagree because ___.” |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | This avoids taking a position. To evaluate, state whether you agree and give a reason. | Esto evita tomar una postura. Para evaluar, di si estás de acuerdo y da una razón. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | Take a position and support it. | Toma una postura y apóyala. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | Right. It states a position, gives the scientific reason (no air resistance), and adds evidence from a real test on the Moon. | Correcto. Expresa una postura, da la razón científica (no hay resistencia del aire) y agrega evidencia de una prueba real en la Luna. |
| items › #4 › context | Social studies: “Why did many early Texas towns grow up along rivers? Justify your answer.” | Estudios sociales: “Why did many early Texas towns grow up along rivers? Justify your answer.” |
| items › #4 › prompt | Which response justifies the answer best? | ¿Qué respuesta justifica mejor la respuesta? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Frames: “I know ___ because ___.” / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” | Marcos: “I know ___ because ___.” (= “Sé que ___ porque ___”) / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” |
| items › #4 › support › medium | Think: what did people need water and rivers for? | Piensa: ¿para qué necesitaba la gente el agua y los ríos? |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | “Rivers are nice to live by.” | “Rivers are nice to live by.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | “Towns grew along rivers because rivers gave people water for farming and a way to move goods, such as cotton, to market.” | “Towns grew along rivers because rivers gave people water for farming and a way to move goods, such as cotton, to market.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | “Towns grew along rivers because that is where the towns were.” | “Towns grew along rivers because that is where the towns were.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | “Nice” is a personal opinion, not a historical reason. Name what rivers provided. | “Nice” es una opinión personal, no una razón histórica. Nombra lo que proporcionaban los ríos. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › hint | Give a specific reason, such as farming or trade. | Da una razón específica, como la agricultura o el comercio. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | Right. It gives two specific reasons (water for farming, transportation for trade) and an example (cotton). | Correcto. Da dos razones específicas (agua para la agricultura y transporte para el comercio) y un ejemplo (el algodón). |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | This is circular — it repeats the claim instead of explaining it. | Esto es circular: repite la afirmación en lugar de explicarla. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › hint | A reason must add new information. | Una razón debe agregar información nueva. |
| items › #5 › context | Math: A classmate says, “The mean of 2, 4, and 9 is 4, because 4 is in the middle.” | Matemáticas: Un compañero dice: “The mean of 2, 4, and 9 is 4, because 4 is in the middle.” |
| items › #5 › prompt | Which response evaluates the claim respectfully and with a reason? | ¿Qué respuesta evalúa la afirmación con respeto y con una razón? |
| items › #5 › support › high | Frames: “I know ___ because ___.” / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” | Marcos: “I know ___ because ___.” (= “Sé que ___ porque ___”) / “The evidence that supports this is ___.” / “I agree / disagree with ___ because ___.” |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Mean = add the numbers and divide by how many there are. The middle number is the median. | Media (mean) = suma los números y divide entre cuántos hay. El número del medio es la mediana (median). |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | “You're wrong, it's 5.” | “You're wrong, it's 5.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | “I agree. The mean is always the middle number.” | “I agree. The mean is always the middle number.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | “I agree that 4 is the middle number, but that is the median. The mean is (2 + 4 + 9) ÷ 3 = 5.” | “I agree that 4 is the middle number, but that is the median. The mean is (2 + 4 + 9) ÷ 3 = 5.” |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | The number is correct, but there is no reason, and “You're wrong” is not a respectful way to evaluate. | El número es correcto, pero no hay razón, y “You're wrong” no es una forma respetuosa de evaluar. |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › hint | Show the calculation and keep the tone respectful. | Muestra el cálculo y mantén un tono respetuoso. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | This repeats the mistake: the middle number is the median, not the mean. | Esto repite el error: el número del medio es la mediana, no la media. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | How do you calculate a mean? | ¿Cómo se calcula la media? |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | Right. It agrees with the part that is true, names the confusion (median vs. mean), and shows the calculation as evidence. | Correcto. Está de acuerdo con la parte que es cierta, nombra la confusión (mediana vs. media) y muestra el cálculo como evidencia. |

### Context Clues for Academic Words  `all-clues-6-8-025` — 77 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Context Clues for Academic Words | Pistas de contexto para palabras académicas |
| studentGoal | Use context clues — definition, example, contrast, cause and effect, analogy, and clues beyond the sentence — to figure out academic words in science and social studies texts. | Usa pistas de contexto —definición, ejemplo, contraste, causa y efecto, analogía y pistas fuera de la oración— para descubrir palabras académicas en textos de ciencias y estudios sociales. |
| studentDirections | Read the sentence and find the clue around the target word. Choose the meaning that fits. Choose, then select Check. | Lee la oración y encuentra la pista alrededor de la palabra meta. Elige el significado que encaja. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Each item models one context-clue type named in the grade 6–8 TEKS: definition and example (grade 6), contrast and cause-and-effect (grade 7), analogy (grade 6), and clues beyond the sentence (grade 8). Have students name the clue type (the CLEAR “lens”) and the signal words: which means, such as, unlike, because, is to. More support shows the Spanish cognate for each word — every target word has one. | Cada ejercicio modela un tipo de pista de contexto de los TEKS de 6.º a 8.º: definición y ejemplo (6.º), contraste y causa y efecto (7.º), analogía (6.º) y pistas fuera de la oración (8.º). Pide a los estudiantes que nombren el tipo de pista (el “lente” de CLEAR) y las palabras señal: which means, such as, unlike, because, is to. Más apoyo muestra el cognado en español de cada palabra; todas las palabras meta tienen uno. |
| languageFunction | Using context clues and cognates to determine the meaning of academic vocabulary. | Usar pistas de contexto y cognados para determinar el significado del vocabulario académico. |
| items › #1 › context | Science: The soil in the region is arid, which means it gets very little rain and stays dry most of the year. | Ciencias: The soil in the region is arid, which means it gets very little rain and stays dry most of the year. |
| items › #1 › prompt | What does “arid” mean here? | ¿Qué significa “arid” aquí? |
| items › #1 › support › high | Clue type: definition (“which means”). Spanish cognate: árido. | Tipo de pista: definición (“which means” = “lo que significa”). Cognado en español: árido. |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Read the words right after “which means.” | Lee las palabras justo después de “which means.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | very wet | muy húmedo |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | very dry | muy seco |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | very rocky | muy rocoso |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | The sentence says the soil gets very little rain and stays dry — the opposite of wet. | La oración dice que el suelo recibe muy poca lluvia y se mantiene seco: lo contrario de húmedo. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › hint | What does the sentence say after “which means”? | ¿Qué dice la oración después de “which means”? |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | Right. “Which means” signals a definition: little rain, dry most of the year. Arid = árido. | Correcto. “Which means” señala una definición: poca lluvia, seco casi todo el año. Arid = árido. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | Nothing in the sentence mentions rocks. The definition clue is about rain and dryness. | Nada en la oración menciona rocas. La pista de definición trata de la lluvia y la sequedad. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › hint | Use only what the sentence tells you. | Usa solo lo que te dice la oración. |
| items › #2 › context | Science: Herbivores, such as deer, rabbits, and cows, eat only plants. | Ciencias: Herbivores, such as deer, rabbits, and cows, eat only plants. |
| items › #2 › prompt | What are “herbivores”? | ¿Qué son los “herbivores”? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Clue type: example (“such as”). Spanish cognate: herbívoros. | Tipo de pista: ejemplo (“such as” = “como”). Cognado en español: herbívoros. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | What do deer, rabbits, and cows have in common? | ¿Qué tienen en común los venados, los conejos y las vacas? |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | animals that eat other animals | animales que comen otros animales |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | animals that live on farms | animales que viven en granjas |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | animals that eat only plants | animales que solo comen plantas |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Deer, rabbits, and cows do not eat other animals, and the sentence says “eat only plants.” | Los venados, los conejos y las vacas no comen otros animales, y la oración dice “eat only plants.” |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › hint | Look at the examples and the end of the sentence. | Mira los ejemplos y el final de la oración. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | Cows live on farms, but deer and rabbits usually live in the wild. The examples share their diet, not where they live. | Las vacas viven en granjas, pero los venados y los conejos suelen vivir en la naturaleza. Los ejemplos comparten su dieta, no dónde viven. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | What do ALL three examples share? | ¿Qué comparten LOS TRES ejemplos? |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | Right. “Such as” introduces examples, and all three eat only plants. Herbivores = herbívoros. | Correcto. “Such as” introduce ejemplos, y los tres comen solo plantas. Herbivores = herbívoros. |
| items › #3 › context | Science: Unlike clear glass, which lets light pass through, the wooden door is opaque. | Ciencias: Unlike clear glass, which lets light pass through, the wooden door is opaque. |
| items › #3 › prompt | What does “opaque” mean here? | ¿Qué significa “opaque” aquí? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Clue type: contrast (“unlike”). Spanish cognate: opaco. | Tipo de pista: contraste (“unlike” = “a diferencia de”). Cognado en español: opaco. |
| items › #3 › support › medium | “Unlike” tells you the door is the OPPOSITE of clear glass. | “Unlike” te dice que la puerta es lo CONTRARIO del vidrio transparente. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | does not let light pass through | no deja pasar la luz |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | shiny and smooth | brillante y liso |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | easy to break | fácil de romper |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | Right. “Unlike” signals contrast: glass lets light through, so the opaque door does not. Opaque = opaco. | Correcto. “Unlike” señala un contraste: el vidrio deja pasar la luz, así que la puerta opaca no. Opaque = opaco. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | The contrast is about light passing through, not about how the surface looks or feels. | El contraste trata de si pasa la luz, no de cómo se ve o se siente la superficie. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | What does glass do that the door does not? | ¿Qué hace el vidrio que la puerta no hace? |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | Glass breaks easily, but the sentence contrasts the two by light, not by strength. | El vidrio se rompe fácilmente, pero la oración los contrasta por la luz, no por la resistencia. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › hint | Find the words after “which.” | Busca las palabras después de “which.” |
| items › #4 › context | Social studies: Because the old bridge was unstable, engineers closed it until it could be repaired. | Estudios sociales: Because the old bridge was unstable, engineers closed it until it could be repaired. |
| items › #4 › prompt | What does “unstable” mean here? | ¿Qué significa “unstable” aquí? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Clue type: cause and effect (“because”). Spanish cognate: inestable. | Tipo de pista: causa y efecto (“because” = “porque”). Cognado en español: inestable. |
| items › #4 › support › medium | The effect is that engineers closed the bridge. What cause would make them do that? | El efecto es que los ingenieros cerraron el puente. ¿Qué causa los llevaría a hacer eso? |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | brand new | completamente nuevo |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | not firm; likely to move or fall | poco firme; que puede moverse o caerse |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | too crowded | demasiado lleno de gente |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | A brand-new bridge would not need repairs. The sentence says it was old and needed fixing. | Un puente nuevo no necesitaría reparaciones. La oración dice que era viejo y había que arreglarlo. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › hint | Why would engineers close a bridge to repair it? | ¿Por qué cerrarían los ingenieros un puente para repararlo? |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | Right. “Because” links cause and effect: the bridge was not firm, so it was closed for repair. Unstable = inestable (un- = in-, “not”). | Correcto. “Because” une causa y efecto: el puente no era firme, así que lo cerraron para repararlo. Unstable = inestable (un- = in-, “no”). |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | Crowding is not fixed by repairs. The clue “until it could be repaired” points to a problem with the bridge itself. | La cantidad de gente no se arregla con reparaciones. La pista “until it could be repaired” apunta a un problema del puente mismo. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › hint | Notice “until it could be repaired.” | Fíjate en “until it could be repaired.” |
| items › #5 › context | Social studies: A tributary is to a river as a branch is to a tree trunk. | Estudios sociales: A tributary is to a river as a branch is to a tree trunk. |
| items › #5 › prompt | What is a “tributary”? | ¿Qué es un “tributary”? |
| items › #5 › support › high | Clue type: analogy (“is to … as … is to”). Spanish: afluente or río tributario. | Tipo de pista: analogía (“is to … as … is to”). En español: afluente o río tributario. |
| items › #5 › support › medium | A branch is a smaller part that joins the trunk. Apply that to a river. | Una rama es una parte más pequeña que se une al tronco. Aplica eso a un río. |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | a smaller stream that flows into a larger river | un arroyo más pequeño que desemboca en un río más grande |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | the place where a river meets the ocean | el lugar donde un río llega al océano |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | a wall built across a river | un muro construido a lo ancho de un río |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | Right. A branch joins a trunk the way a smaller stream joins a larger river. Tributary = tributario / afluente. | Correcto. Una rama se une al tronco como un arroyo más pequeño se une a un río más grande. Tributary = tributario / afluente. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | That is a river's mouth (desembocadura). The analogy compares a smaller part joining a bigger one, like a branch and a trunk. | Eso es la desembocadura de un río. La analogía compara una parte pequeña que se une a una más grande, como una rama y un tronco. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | How does a branch relate to a trunk? | ¿Qué relación tiene una rama con el tronco? |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | That is a dam (presa). A branch is a natural part that connects to the trunk, not something built to block it. | Eso es una presa (dam). Una rama es una parte natural que se conecta al tronco, no algo construido para bloquearlo. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › hint | Follow the analogy: branch → trunk. | Sigue la analogía: rama → tronco. |
| items › #6 › context | Social studies: The colonists refused to buy British tea. The boycott lasted for months, and merchants in London lost a great deal of money. | Estudios sociales: The colonists refused to buy British tea. The boycott lasted for months, and merchants in London lost a great deal of money. |
| items › #6 › prompt | What is a “boycott”? | ¿Qué es un “boycott”? |
| items › #6 › support › high | Clue type: beyond the sentence — read the sentence BEFORE the word. Spanish cognate: boicot. | Tipo de pista: fuera de la oración; lee la oración ANTERIOR a la palabra. Cognado en español: boicot. |
| items › #6 › support › medium | “The boycott” refers back to something in the first sentence. | “The boycott” se refiere a algo de la primera oración. |
| items › #6 › choices › #1 › label | a tax on imported goods | un impuesto sobre productos importados |
| items › #6 › choices › #2 › label | a trade agreement between two countries | un acuerdo comercial entre dos países |
| items › #6 › choices › #3 › label | refusing to buy something as a form of protest | negarse a comprar algo como forma de protesta |
| items › #6 › feedback › a › why | A tax would make merchants collect money, not lose it. The first sentence says the colonists refused to buy. | Un impuesto haría que se cobrara dinero, no que se perdiera. La primera oración dice que los colonos se negaron a comprar. |
| items › #6 › feedback › a › hint | Reread the first sentence. | Vuelve a leer la primera oración. |
| items › #6 › feedback › b › why | An agreement means both sides cooperate. Here the colonists refused to buy, and London merchants lost money. | Un acuerdo significa que ambos lados cooperan. Aquí los colonos se negaron a comprar, y los comerciantes de Londres perdieron dinero. |
| items › #6 › feedback › b › hint | What did the colonists refuse to do? | ¿Qué se negaron a hacer los colonos? |
| items › #6 › feedback › c › why | Right. “The boycott” points back to the colonists refusing to buy tea, and the next clause shows its effect. Boycott = boicot. | Correcto. “The boycott” se refiere a que los colonos se negaron a comprar té, y lo que sigue muestra su efecto. Boycott = boicot. |

### Talk Like a Mathematician, Scientist, or Historian  `all-content-6-8-026` — 56 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Talk Like a Mathematician, Scientist, or Historian | Habla como matemático, científico o historiador |
| studentGoal | Recognize and use the discussion language of each content area — how mathematicians, scientists, and historians talk about their thinking. | Reconoce y usa el lenguaje de discusión de cada materia: cómo hablan de su razonamiento los matemáticos, los científicos y los historiadores. |
| studentDirections | Read each discussion sentence. Decide which class you would most likely hear it in: math, science, or social studies. Place each sentence, then select Check. | Lee cada oración de discusión. Decide en qué clase es más probable escucharla: matemáticas, ciencias o estudios sociales. Coloca cada oración y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Each content area has its own ways of talking: mathematicians represent and check, scientists hypothesize and infer, historians cite sources and weigh perspectives. After sorting, have students choose one sentence from each bin, turn it into a frame (“According to this primary source, ___”), and say it aloud in their next discussion in that class. Many of the key words have Spanish cognates (hipótesis, variable, perspectiva, fuente primaria) — More support shows them. | Cada materia tiene su propia forma de hablar: los matemáticos representan y comprueban, los científicos plantean hipótesis e infieren, los historiadores citan fuentes y consideran perspectivas. Después de clasificar, pide a los estudiantes que elijan una oración de cada categoría, la conviertan en un marco (“According to this primary source, ___”) y la digan en voz alta en su próxima discusión en esa clase. Muchas palabras clave tienen cognados en español (hipótesis, variable, perspectiva, fuente primaria); Más apoyo los muestra. |
| languageFunction | Classifying and rehearsing discipline-specific discussion language. | Clasificar y practicar el lenguaje de discusión propio de cada materia. |
| categories › #1 › label | Math talk | Lenguaje de matemáticas |
| categories › #1 › hint | Equations, representations, patterns, and checking answers. | Ecuaciones, representaciones, patrones y comprobar respuestas. |
| categories › #2 › label | Science talk | Lenguaje de ciencias |
| categories › #2 › hint | Hypotheses, variables, observations, and data. | Hipótesis, variables, observaciones y datos. |
| categories › #3 › label | Social studies talk | Lenguaje de estudios sociales |
| categories › #3 › hint | Sources, causes and effects, and perspectives. | Fuentes, causas y efectos, y perspectivas. |
| items › #1 › prompt | “I can check my answer by substituting it back into the equation.” | “I can check my answer by substituting it back into the equation.” |
| items › #1 › support › high | Spanish: “Puedo comprobar mi respuesta sustituyéndola en la ecuación.” | “Puedo comprobar mi respuesta sustituyéndola en la ecuación.” |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Key words: substituting, equation. | Palabras clave: substituting (sustituir), equation (ecuación). |
| items › #1 › feedback › math › why | Substituting a value back into an equation is how mathematicians check a solution. | Sustituir un valor en una ecuación es la manera en que los matemáticos comprueban una solución. |
| items › #1 › feedback › math › hint | Which subject checks answers with equations and patterns? | ¿Qué materia comprueba respuestas con ecuaciones y patrones? |
| items › #2 › prompt | “Our hypothesis was that plants grow taller with more light, and the data supported it.” | “Our hypothesis was that plants grow taller with more light, and the data supported it.” |
| items › #2 › support › high | Spanish: “Nuestra hipótesis era que las plantas crecen más con más luz, y los datos la apoyaron.” | “Nuestra hipótesis era que las plantas crecen más con más luz, y los datos la apoyaron.” |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Key words: hypothesis, data. | Palabras clave: hypothesis (hipótesis), data (datos). |
| items › #2 › feedback › science › why | Stating a hypothesis and whether the data supported it is core science discussion language. | Expresar una hipótesis y si los datos la apoyaron es lenguaje central de las discusiones de ciencias. |
| items › #2 › feedback › science › hint | Which subject tests hypotheses and collects data? | ¿Qué materia pone a prueba hipótesis y recoge datos? |
| items › #3 › prompt | “According to this primary source, the settlers were worried about a shortage of food.” | “According to this primary source, the settlers were worried about a shortage of food.” |
| items › #3 › support › high | Spanish: “Según esta fuente primaria, los colonos estaban preocupados por la escasez de comida.” | “Según esta fuente primaria, los colonos estaban preocupados por la escasez de comida.” |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Key words: primary source. | Palabras clave: primary source (fuente primaria). |
| items › #3 › feedback › social › why | Historians cite primary sources — documents from the time — to support what they say about the past. | Los historiadores citan fuentes primarias (documentos de la época) para apoyar lo que dicen sobre el pasado. |
| items › #3 › feedback › social › hint | Which subject studies sources, causes, and perspectives in history? | ¿Qué materia estudia fuentes, causas y perspectivas en la historia? |
| items › #4 › prompt | “Another way to represent this relationship is with a table of values.” | “Another way to represent this relationship is with a table of values.” |
| items › #4 › support › high | Spanish: “Otra manera de representar esta relación es con una tabla de valores.” | “Otra manera de representar esta relación es con una tabla de valores.” |
| items › #4 › support › medium | Key words: represent, table of values. | Palabras clave: represent (representar), table of values (tabla de valores). |
| items › #4 › feedback › math › why | Showing the same relationship with a table, graph, or equation is a key move in math discussion. | Mostrar la misma relación con una tabla, una gráfica o una ecuación es un movimiento clave en las discusiones de matemáticas. |
| items › #4 › feedback › math › hint | Which subject checks answers with equations and patterns? | ¿Qué materia comprueba respuestas con ecuaciones y patrones? |
| items › #5 › prompt | “The independent variable in our investigation was the temperature of the water.” | “The independent variable in our investigation was the temperature of the water.” |
| items › #5 › support › high | Spanish: “La variable independiente de nuestra investigación fue la temperatura del agua.” | “La variable independiente de nuestra investigación fue la temperatura del agua.” |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Key words: independent variable, investigation. | Palabras clave: independent variable (variable independiente), investigation (investigación). |
| items › #5 › feedback › science › why | The independent variable is the factor a scientist changes on purpose in an investigation. | La variable independiente es el factor que un científico cambia a propósito en una investigación. |
| items › #5 › feedback › science › hint | Which subject tests hypotheses and collects data? | ¿Qué materia pone a prueba hipótesis y recoge datos? |
| items › #6 › prompt | “One cause of the conflict was competition for land, and one effect was a new treaty.” | “One cause of the conflict was competition for land, and one effect was a new treaty.” |
| items › #6 › support › high | Spanish: “Una causa del conflicto fue la competencia por la tierra, y un efecto fue un nuevo tratado.” | “Una causa del conflicto fue la competencia por la tierra, y un efecto fue un nuevo tratado.” |
| items › #6 › support › medium | Key words: cause, conflict, effect, treaty. | Palabras clave: cause (causa), conflict (conflicto), effect (efecto), treaty (tratado). |
| items › #6 › feedback › social › why | Explaining the causes and effects of historical events, such as conflicts and treaties, is social studies reasoning. | Explicar las causas y los efectos de sucesos históricos, como conflictos y tratados, es razonamiento de estudios sociales. |
| items › #6 › feedback › social › hint | Which subject studies sources, causes, and perspectives in history? | ¿Qué materia estudia fuentes, causas y perspectivas en la historia? |
| items › #7 › prompt | “The pattern increases by 4 each time, so the rule is y = 4x.” | “The pattern increases by 4 each time, so the rule is y = 4x.” |
| items › #7 › support › high | Spanish: “El patrón aumenta de 4 en 4, así que la regla es y = 4x.” | “El patrón aumenta de 4 en 4, así que la regla es y = 4x.” |
| items › #7 › support › medium | Key words: pattern, rule. | Palabras clave: pattern (patrón), rule (regla). |
| items › #7 › feedback › math › why | Describing a pattern and writing its rule as an equation is algebraic thinking. | Describir un patrón y escribir su regla como ecuación es pensamiento algebraico. |
| items › #7 › feedback › math › hint | Which subject checks answers with equations and patterns? | ¿Qué materia comprueba respuestas con ecuaciones y patrones? |
| items › #8 › prompt | “We observed bubbles forming, so we can infer that a gas was produced.” | “We observed bubbles forming, so we can infer that a gas was produced.” |
| items › #8 › support › high | Spanish: “Observamos que se formaban burbujas, así que podemos inferir que se produjo un gas.” | “Observamos que se formaban burbujas, así que podemos inferir que se produjo un gas.” |
| items › #8 › support › medium | Key words: observed, infer. | Palabras clave: observed (observamos), infer (inferir). |
| items › #8 › feedback › science › why | Moving from an observation to an inference is how scientists reason from evidence in a lab. | Pasar de una observación a una inferencia es la manera en que los científicos razonan a partir de la evidencia en el laboratorio. |
| items › #8 › feedback › science › hint | Which subject tests hypotheses and collects data? | ¿Qué materia pone a prueba hipótesis y recoge datos? |
| items › #9 › prompt | “From the perspective of the farmers, the new railroad meant they could sell crops in distant cities.” | “From the perspective of the farmers, the new railroad meant they could sell crops in distant cities.” |

| items › #9 › support › medium | Key words: perspective. | Palabras clave: perspective (perspectiva). |
| items › #9 › feedback › social › why | Considering how a historical event looked to different groups — their perspective — is social studies thinking. | Considerar cómo vieron un suceso histórico distintos grupos (su perspectiva) es pensamiento de estudios sociales. |
| items › #9 › feedback › social › hint | Which subject studies sources, causes, and perspectives in history? | ¿Qué materia estudia fuentes, causas y perspectivas en la historia? |

### Transitions That Explain  `all-transitions-6-8-027` — 65 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Transitions That Explain | Transiciones que explican |
| studentGoal | Choose the transition word that shows how two ideas connect — cause and effect, example, contrast, sequence, or comparison — in a written explanation. | Elige la palabra de transición que muestra cómo se conectan dos ideas —causa y efecto, ejemplo, contraste, secuencia o comparación— en una explicación escrita. |
| studentDirections | Read the two sentences. Decide how the second idea connects to the first, then choose the transition that fits the blank. Choose, then select Check. | Lee las dos oraciones. Decide cómo se conecta la segunda idea con la primera y elige la transición que completa el espacio. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Transitions are not interchangeable: each one signals a relationship. Before choosing, have students name the relationship (cause/effect, example, contrast, sequence, comparison). The distractors are real transitions that signal the wrong relationship. Extend by having students write a three-sentence explanation in their current science or social studies unit using two transitions from the bank. | Las transiciones no son intercambiables: cada una señala una relación. Antes de elegir, pide a los estudiantes que nombren la relación (causa/efecto, ejemplo, contraste, secuencia, comparación). Los distractores son transiciones reales que señalan la relación equivocada. Para ampliar, pide que escriban una explicación de tres oraciones sobre su unidad actual de ciencias o estudios sociales usando dos transiciones del banco. |
| languageFunction | Connecting ideas in written explanations with transitions that signal relationships. | Conectar ideas en explicaciones escritas con transiciones que señalan relaciones. |
| items › #1 › context | Science: “Plants need sunlight to make their own food. ___, a plant kept in a dark closet for weeks will grow weak.” | Ciencias: “Plants need sunlight to make their own food. ___, a plant kept in a dark closet for weeks will grow weak.” |
| items › #1 › prompt | Which transition fits the blank? | ¿Qué transición completa el espacio? |
| items › #1 › support › high | Transition bank: as a result (cause → effect) · for example (example) · however (contrast) · next (sequence) · similarly (comparison). | Banco de transiciones: as a result (= como resultado; causa → efecto) · for example (= por ejemplo) · however (= sin embargo; contraste) · next (= luego; secuencia) · similarly (= de manera similar; comparación). |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Is the second sentence a result of the first? | ¿La segunda oración es un resultado de la primera? |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | However | However |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | For example | For example |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | As a result | As a result |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | “However” signals a contrast, but the second sentence does not contradict the first — it follows from it. | “However” señala un contraste, pero la segunda oración no contradice la primera: es una consecuencia de ella. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › hint | Is this a contrast or a result? | ¿Es un contraste o un resultado? |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | A plant growing weak is not an example of needing sunlight; it is what happens without it. | Que una planta se debilite no es un ejemplo de necesitar luz; es lo que pasa sin ella. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › hint | Look for a cause-and-effect transition. | Busca una transición de causa y efecto. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | Right. No sunlight (cause) → weak plant (effect). “As a result” signals cause and effect. | Correcto. Sin luz solar (causa) → planta débil (efecto). “As a result” señala causa y efecto. |
| items › #2 › context | Science: “Many animals have adaptations for cold climates. ___, the arctic fox grows a thick white coat in winter.” | Ciencias: “Many animals have adaptations for cold climates. ___, the arctic fox grows a thick white coat in winter.” |
| items › #2 › prompt | Which transition fits the blank? | ¿Qué transición completa el espacio? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Transition bank: as a result (cause → effect) · for example (example) · however (contrast) · next (sequence) · similarly (comparison). | Banco de transiciones: as a result (= como resultado; causa → efecto) · for example (= por ejemplo) · however (= sin embargo; contraste) · next (= luego; secuencia) · similarly (= de manera similar; comparación). |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Is the arctic fox one case of the general idea? | ¿El zorro ártico es un caso de la idea general? |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | For example | For example |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | Instead | Instead |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | In conclusion | In conclusion |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Right. The first sentence is general; the arctic fox is a specific case. “For example” introduces it. | Correcto. La primera oración es general; el zorro ártico es un caso específico. “For example” lo introduce. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | “Instead” signals a replacement, but the fox does not replace the first idea — it illustrates it. | “Instead” señala un reemplazo, pero el zorro no reemplaza la primera idea: la ilustra. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | General idea → specific case. Which transition fits? | Idea general → caso específico. ¿Qué transición encaja? |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | “In conclusion” wraps up a whole explanation. Here the writer is just starting to give a specific case. | “In conclusion” cierra toda una explicación. Aquí el autor apenas empieza a dar un caso específico. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › hint | The second sentence gives one case. | La segunda oración da un caso. |
| items › #3 › context | Social studies: “Spanish explorers came to Texas hoping to find gold. ___, they found few riches and faced many hardships.” | Estudios sociales: “Spanish explorers came to Texas hoping to find gold. ___, they found few riches and faced many hardships.” |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which transition fits the blank? | ¿Qué transición completa el espacio? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Transition bank: as a result (cause → effect) · for example (example) · however (contrast) · next (sequence) · similarly (comparison). | Banco de transiciones: as a result (= como resultado; causa → efecto) · for example (= por ejemplo) · however (= sin embargo; contraste) · next (= luego; secuencia) · similarly (= de manera similar; comparación). |
| items › #3 › support › medium | What they hoped for and what happened are different. | Lo que esperaban y lo que pasó son diferentes. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | Therefore | Therefore |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | However | However |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | Similarly | Similarly |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | “Therefore” signals a result, but hoping for gold did not cause them to find little. The ideas contrast. | “Therefore” señala un resultado, pero esperar oro no causó que encontraran poco. Las ideas contrastan. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › hint | Hope vs. reality — what relationship is that? | Expectativa vs. realidad: ¿qué relación es esa? |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | Right. What they expected (gold) contrasts with what happened (few riches). “However” signals that contrast. | Correcto. Lo que esperaban (oro) contrasta con lo que pasó (pocas riquezas). “However” señala ese contraste. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | “Similarly” signals that two things are alike, but these ideas are opposites. | “Similarly” señala que dos cosas se parecen, pero estas ideas son opuestas. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › hint | Are the two ideas alike or different? | ¿Las dos ideas se parecen o son diferentes? |
| items › #4 › context | Math: “To find the area of a rectangle, first measure the length and the width. ___, multiply the length by the width.” | Matemáticas: “To find the area of a rectangle, first measure the length and the width. ___, multiply the length by the width.” |
| items › #4 › prompt | Which transition fits the blank? | ¿Qué transición completa el espacio? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Transition bank: as a result (cause → effect) · for example (example) · however (contrast) · next (sequence) · similarly (comparison). | Banco de transiciones: as a result (= como resultado; causa → efecto) · for example (= por ejemplo) · however (= sin embargo; contraste) · next (= luego; secuencia) · similarly (= de manera similar; comparación). |
| items › #4 › support › medium | The first sentence says “first.” What comes after first? | La primera oración dice “first”. ¿Qué viene después de “first”? |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | Although | Although |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | For instance | For instance |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | Next | Next |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | “Although” introduces a contrast, but these are steps in order. | “Although” introduce un contraste, pero estos son pasos en orden. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › hint | Look for a sequence word. | Busca una palabra de secuencia. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | “For instance” introduces an example, but multiplying is the next step, not an example. | “For instance” introduce un ejemplo, pero multiplicar es el siguiente paso, no un ejemplo. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › hint | These are steps. Which word shows order? | Son pasos. ¿Qué palabra muestra el orden? |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | Right. “First … next” shows the order of steps in a procedure. | Correcto. “First … next” muestra el orden de los pasos de un procedimiento. |
| items › #5 › context | Math: “The mean is one way to describe the center of a data set. ___, the median also describes the center of a data set.” | Matemáticas: “The mean is one way to describe the center of a data set. ___, the median also describes the center of a data set.” |
| items › #5 › prompt | Which transition fits the blank? | ¿Qué transición completa el espacio? |
| items › #5 › support › high | Transition bank: as a result (cause → effect) · for example (example) · however (contrast) · next (sequence) · similarly (comparison). | Banco de transiciones: as a result (= como resultado; causa → efecto) · for example (= por ejemplo) · however (= sin embargo; contraste) · next (= luego; secuencia) · similarly (= de manera similar; comparación). |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Both sentences say the same kind of thing about two measures. | Las dos oraciones dicen lo mismo sobre dos medidas. |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | As a result | As a result |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | Similarly | Similarly |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | Otherwise | Otherwise |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | The median does not describe the center BECAUSE of the mean. There is no cause and effect here. | La mediana no describe el centro POR CAUSA de la media. Aquí no hay causa y efecto. |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › hint | Are the two measures being compared? | ¿Se están comparando las dos medidas? |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | Right. Both the mean and the median describe the center, so “Similarly” signals the comparison. | Correcto. Tanto la media como la mediana describen el centro, así que “Similarly” señala la comparación. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | “Otherwise” means “if not,” which does not connect these two ideas. | “Otherwise” significa “si no”, lo cual no conecta estas dos ideas. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › hint | Notice the word “also.” | Fíjate en la palabra “also.” |

### Revise for Academic Register  `all-response-9-12-028` — 65 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Revise for Academic Register | Revisa para usar un registro académico |
| studentGoal | Revise sentences in your own academic responses so the register, vocabulary, and tone fit an essay, lab report, or constructed response — without sounding stiff or inflated. | Revisa oraciones de tus propias respuestas académicas para que el registro, el vocabulario y el tono sean adecuados para un ensayo, un informe de laboratorio o una respuesta construida, sin sonar rígido ni rebuscado. |
| studentDirections | Each item shows a draft sentence from a student's academic response. Choose the best revision. Choose, then select Check. | Cada ejercicio muestra una oración en borrador de la respuesta académica de un estudiante. Elige la mejor revisión. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | This activity follows “Formal or Informal?”: there students matched register to a situation; here every item is already an academic response, and the work is revising inside it. Each item targets one habit: vague “I think … kinda,” second-person “you,” slang and contractions, emotional tone in a lab report, and dismissing a counterargument. Note the inflated distractors — academic register is precise, not pompous. Have students apply the same moves to a paragraph from their own recent writing. | Esta actividad sigue a “Formal or Informal?”: allí los estudiantes adaptaban el registro a una situación; aquí cada ejercicio ya es una respuesta académica, y el trabajo es revisar dentro de ella. Cada ejercicio trata un hábito: el vago “I think … kinda”, la segunda persona “you”, la jerga y las contracciones, el tono emotivo en un informe de laboratorio y el rechazo de un contraargumento. Fíjate en los distractores rebuscados: el registro académico es preciso, no pomposo. Pide a los estudiantes que apliquen los mismos cambios a un párrafo de su propia escritura reciente. |
| languageFunction | Revising academic writing for appropriate register, vocabulary, tone, and voice. | Revisar la escritura académica para lograr un registro, vocabulario, tono y voz apropiados. |
| items › #1 › context | Short constructed response about a poem. Draft: “I think the poem is kinda sad.” | Respuesta construida corta sobre un poema. Borrador: “I think the poem is kinda sad.” |
| items › #1 › prompt | Which revision fits an academic response? | ¿Qué revisión es adecuada para una respuesta académica? |
| items › #1 › support › high | Name the device and the effect: “The poem's ___ creates a ___ tone.” / Nombra el recurso y su efecto. | Nombra el recurso y su efecto: “The poem's ___ creates a ___ tone.” |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Replace “kinda sad” with a precise tone word and point to the text. | Cambia “kinda sad” por una palabra de tono precisa y señala el texto. |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | “The poem is super depressing, not gonna lie.” | “The poem is super depressing, not gonna lie.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | “The poem's images of empty rooms create a mournful tone.” | “The poem's images of empty rooms create a mournful tone.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | “In my own humble and personal opinion, the poem is perhaps somewhat sad in a certain way.” | “In my own humble and personal opinion, the poem is perhaps somewhat sad in a certain way.” |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | “Super” and “not gonna lie” are conversational slang, and the sentence still gives no evidence from the poem. | “Super” y “not gonna lie” son jerga conversacional, y la oración sigue sin dar evidencia del poema. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › hint | Which choice names something in the poem? | ¿Qué opción nombra algo del poema? |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | Right. “Mournful” is a precise tone word, and “images of empty rooms” points to evidence in the text. | Correcto. “Mournful” es una palabra de tono precisa, e “images of empty rooms” señala evidencia del texto. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | More words do not make it more academic. Piling up “humble,” “personal,” “perhaps,” and “somewhat” makes the point weaker, not more formal. | Más palabras no lo hacen más académico. Acumular “humble”, “personal”, “perhaps” y “somewhat” debilita la idea, no la hace más formal. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › hint | Academic means precise, not wordy. | Académico significa preciso, no palabrero. |
| items › #2 › context | Article analysis. Draft: “When you read the article, you can tell the author doesn't trust the company.” | Análisis de un artículo. Borrador: “When you read the article, you can tell the author doesn't trust the company.” |
| items › #2 › prompt | Which revision fits an academic response? | ¿Qué revisión es adecuada para una respuesta académica? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Avoid “you.” Make the author or the text the subject: “The author's use of ___ signals ___.” | Evita “you”. Haz que el autor o el texto sea el sujeto: “The author's use of ___ signals ___.” |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Who is the sentence about — the reader or the author? | ¿De quién trata la oración: del lector o del autor? |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | “The author's repeated use of the word ‘claims’ signals distrust of the company.” | “The author's repeated use of the word ‘claims’ signals distrust of the company.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | “You can totally tell the author hates the company.” | “You can totally tell the author hates the company.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | “As you, the reader, will surely agree, you can tell the author doesn't trust them.” | “As you, the reader, will surely agree, you can tell the author doesn't trust them.” |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Right. The author becomes the subject, “you” disappears, and the word choice “claims” is the evidence for the inference. | Correcto. El autor pasa a ser el sujeto, desaparece “you” y la palabra “claims” es la evidencia de la inferencia. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | “You,” “totally,” and “hates” keep the casual register and exaggerate the author's attitude. | “You”, “totally” y “hates” mantienen el registro casual y exageran la actitud del autor. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Remove “you” and point to the author's words. | Quita “you” y señala las palabras del autor. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | This sounds more formal but uses “you” three times and assumes the reader agrees instead of showing evidence. | Esto suena más formal, pero usa “you” tres veces y supone que el lector está de acuerdo en lugar de mostrar evidencia. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › hint | Make the author the subject. | Haz que el autor sea el sujeto. |
| items › #3 › context | History short answer. Draft: “The treaty didn't work out ’cause neither side trusted the other.” | Respuesta corta de historia. Borrador: “The treaty didn't work out ’cause neither side trusted the other.” |
| items › #3 › prompt | Which revision fits an academic response? | ¿Qué revisión es adecuada para una respuesta académica? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Swap the casual words: “didn't work out” → “failed”; “’cause” → “because.” | Cambia las palabras casuales: “didn't work out” → “failed” (fracasó); “’cause” → “because” (porque). |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Keep the idea; change only the casual words. | Mantén la idea; cambia solo las palabras casuales. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | “The treaty was a total flop.” | “The treaty was a total flop.” |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | “The aforementioned treaty was rendered ineffectual by virtue of the mutual and reciprocal absence of trust.” | “The aforementioned treaty was rendered ineffectual by virtue of the mutual and reciprocal absence of trust.” |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | “The treaty failed because neither side trusted the other.” | “The treaty failed because neither side trusted the other.” |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | “A total flop” is slang, and the sentence drops the reason (lack of trust). | “A total flop” es jerga, y la oración elimina la razón (la falta de confianza). |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › hint | Keep the reason and use precise words. | Mantén la razón y usa palabras precisas. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | This is inflated: “aforementioned,” “rendered ineffectual,” and “mutual and reciprocal” (which repeat each other) make a simple idea hard to read. | Esto es rebuscado: “aforementioned”, “rendered ineffectual” y “mutual and reciprocal” (que se repiten) hacen difícil de leer una idea sencilla. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | Clear and precise beats long and fancy. | Claro y preciso es mejor que largo y elegante. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | Right. “Failed” and “because” replace the casual words, and the sentence stays clear and direct. | Correcto. “Failed” y “because” reemplazan las palabras casuales, y la oración queda clara y directa. |
| items › #4 › context | Lab report conclusion. Draft: “Our experiment was a fail, lol, the plant died.” | Conclusión de un informe de laboratorio. Borrador: “Our experiment was a fail, lol, the plant died.” |
| items › #4 › prompt | Which revision fits a lab report? | ¿Qué revisión es adecuada para un informe de laboratorio? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Lab reports state results objectively: “The results did / did not support our hypothesis; ___.” | Los informes de laboratorio presentan los resultados con objetividad: “The results did / did not support our hypothesis; ___.” |
| items › #4 › support › medium | Report what happened and connect it to the hypothesis, without emotion. | Informa lo que pasó y relaciónalo con la hipótesis, sin emoción. |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | “Sadly, our poor little plant tragically passed away.” | “Sadly, our poor little plant tragically passed away.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | “The results did not support our hypothesis; the plant in the saltwater group died by day five.” | “The results did not support our hypothesis; the plant in the saltwater group died by day five.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | “The plant died. Oh well.” | “The plant died. Oh well.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | “Sadly,” “poor little,” and “tragically” add emotional tone. A lab report reports results objectively. | “Sadly”, “poor little” y “tragically” agregan un tono emotivo. Un informe de laboratorio presenta los resultados con objetividad. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › hint | Remove the feelings; keep the data. | Quita los sentimientos; mantén los datos. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | Right. It connects the result to the hypothesis and gives specific data (saltwater group, day five) in an objective tone. | Correcto. Relaciona el resultado con la hipótesis y da datos específicos (grupo con agua salada, día cinco) con un tono objetivo. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | “Oh well” is casual, and the sentence gives no data or link to the hypothesis. | “Oh well” es casual, y la oración no da datos ni relación con la hipótesis. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › hint | Which choice mentions the hypothesis? | ¿Qué opción menciona la hipótesis? |
| items › #5 › context | Argument essay. Draft: “People who disagree with me are just wrong.” | Ensayo argumentativo. Borrador: “People who disagree with me are just wrong.” |
| items › #5 › prompt | Which revision addresses the other side in an academic tone? | ¿Qué revisión trata el punto de vista contrario con un tono académico? |
| items › #5 › support › high | Frame: “Some argue that ___; however, this view overlooks ___.” | Marco: “Some argue that ___; however, this view overlooks ___.” (= “Algunos sostienen que ___; sin embargo, esta postura pasa por alto ___.”) |
| items › #5 › support › medium | State the other side fairly, then respond to it. | Presenta el otro punto de vista con justicia y luego respóndele. |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | “Some argue that homework builds discipline; however, this view overlooks students who work after school to support their families.” | “Some argue that homework builds discipline; however, this view overlooks students who work after school to support their families.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | “Haters will say homework is good, but whatever.” | “Haters will say homework is good, but whatever.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | “Anyone who disagrees with this position is clearly not thinking.” | “Anyone who disagrees with this position is clearly not thinking.” |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | Right. It states the opposing view fairly, then answers it with a specific reason — a respectful, academic tone. | Correcto. Presenta el punto de vista contrario con justicia y lo responde con una razón específica: un tono respetuoso y académico. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | “Haters” and “whatever” are slang, and the sentence dismisses the other side instead of answering it. | “Haters” y “whatever” son jerga, y la oración descarta el otro punto de vista en lugar de responderle. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | Answer the other side with a reason. | Responde al otro punto de vista con una razón. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | The vocabulary sounds formal, but the tone insults readers who disagree. Register includes respectful tone. | El vocabulario suena formal, pero el tono insulta a los lectores que no están de acuerdo. El registro incluye un tono respetuoso. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › hint | Which choice states the other view fairly? | ¿Qué opción presenta el otro punto de vista con justicia? |

### Idioms in Class  `all-idioms-9-12-029` — 77 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Idioms in Class | Modismos en clase |
| studentGoal | Use context to understand idioms that teachers and texts use in class, and notice when English and Spanish share the same idiom. | Usa el contexto para entender modismos que usan los maestros y los textos en clase, y observa cuándo el inglés y el español comparten el mismo modismo. |
| studentDirections | An idiom means something different from its individual words. Read how the idiom is used, then choose what it really means. Choose, then select Check. | Un modismo significa algo diferente de sus palabras por separado. Lee cómo se usa el modismo y elige lo que realmente significa. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Idioms are hard for multilingual learners because the literal meaning is a trap — each item includes a literal-meaning distractor. Point out the bridges: English and Spanish share “the tip of the iceberg” (la punta del iceberg) and “a double-edged sword” (un arma de doble filo), while others need a different Spanish expression (hit the books ≈ ponerse a estudiar / quemarse las pestañas). Several items are spoken by a teacher or classmate; read those aloud first so students practice understanding idioms they hear. | Los modismos son difíciles para los estudiantes multilingües porque el significado literal es una trampa; cada ejercicio incluye un distractor literal. Señala los puentes: el inglés y el español comparten “the tip of the iceberg” (la punta del iceberg) y “a double-edged sword” (un arma de doble filo), mientras que otros necesitan otra expresión en español (hit the books ≈ ponerse a estudiar / quemarse las pestañas). Varios ejercicios los dice un maestro o un compañero; léelos en voz alta primero para que los estudiantes practiquen entender modismos que escuchan. |
| languageFunction | Interpreting idioms and figurative language from context. | Interpretar modismos y lenguaje figurado a partir del contexto. |
| items › #1 › context | Your teacher says: “The unit test is on Friday, so tonight it's time to hit the books.” | Tu maestra dice: “The unit test is on Friday, so tonight it's time to hit the books.” |
| items › #1 › prompt | What does “hit the books” mean? | ¿Qué significa “hit the books”? |
| items › #1 › support › high | Spanish has a different idiom with the same meaning: ponerse a estudiar, quemarse las pestañas. | El español tiene otro modismo con el mismo significado: ponerse a estudiar, quemarse las pestañas. |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Why would a test on Friday matter tonight? | ¿Por qué importa esta noche un examen el viernes? |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | to study hard | estudiar mucho |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | to return books to the library | devolver libros a la biblioteca |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | to drop your books on the desk | dejar caer los libros sobre el escritorio |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | Right. With a test coming, “hit the books” means to study hard. Spanish says something similar with “quemarse las pestañas.” | Correcto. Con un examen próximo, “hit the books” significa estudiar mucho. En español se dice algo parecido con “quemarse las pestañas.” |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | Nothing in the context mentions the library. The test on Friday is the clue. | Nada en el contexto menciona la biblioteca. El examen del viernes es la pista. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › hint | Connect the idiom to the test. | Relaciona el modismo con el examen. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | That is the literal meaning of “hit.” Idioms do not mean their individual words. | Ese es el significado literal de “hit”. Los modismos no significan sus palabras por separado. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › hint | Think beyond the literal words. | Piensa más allá de las palabras literales. |
| items › #2 › context | History text: “The protest over the new tax was just the tip of the iceberg; anger toward the government had been growing for years.” | Texto de historia: “The protest over the new tax was just the tip of the iceberg; anger toward the government had been growing for years.” |
| items › #2 › prompt | What does “the tip of the iceberg” mean here? | ¿Qué significa “the tip of the iceberg” aquí? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Same idiom in Spanish: “la punta del iceberg.” Most of an iceberg is hidden under water. | Mismo modismo en español: “la punta del iceberg.” La mayor parte de un iceberg está oculta bajo el agua. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | What does “had been growing for years” tell you? | ¿Qué te dice “had been growing for years”? |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | the most important and final event | el suceso más importante y final |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | a dangerous accident at sea | un accidente peligroso en el mar |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | a small, visible part of a much larger problem | una parte pequeña y visible de un problema mucho mayor |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | The sentence says anger had been growing for years — the protest was only a small, visible sign, not the whole story. | La oración dice que el enojo llevaba años creciendo: la protesta era solo una señal pequeña y visible, no toda la historia. |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › hint | Most of an iceberg is hidden. What is hidden here? | La mayor parte de un iceberg está oculta. ¿Qué está oculto aquí? |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | That is the literal meaning. The text is about a protest, not the ocean. | Ese es el significado literal. El texto trata de una protesta, no del océano. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Read the idiom as figurative language. | Lee el modismo como lenguaje figurado. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | Right. Like an iceberg, the protest was the small part people could see; years of anger lay underneath. Spanish uses the same idiom. | Correcto. Como un iceberg, la protesta era la parte pequeña que se veía; debajo había años de enojo. El español usa el mismo modismo. |
| items › #3 › context | In a class debate, a classmate says: “I'm still on the fence about whether the city should build the new stadium.” | En un debate de clase, un compañero dice: “I'm still on the fence about whether the city should build the new stadium.” |
| items › #3 › prompt | What does “on the fence” mean? | ¿Qué significa “on the fence”? |
| items › #3 › support › high | Imagine sitting on a fence between two yards — not on either side. Spanish: estar indeciso. | Imagina estar sentado en una cerca entre dos patios, sin estar de ningún lado. En español: estar indeciso. |
| items › #3 › support › medium | “Still … about whether” is a clue. | “Still … about whether” es una pista. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | strongly against the idea | totalmente en contra de la idea |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | undecided between two sides | indeciso entre dos posturas |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | waiting outside the stadium | esperando afuera del estadio |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | “Still … about whether” shows the speaker has not chosen a side yet — not that they are against it. | “Still … about whether” muestra que la persona todavía no ha elegido una postura, no que esté en contra. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › hint | Has the speaker decided? | ¿La persona ya decidió? |
