| items › #5 › prompt | Where do you go? | ¿A dónde vas? |
| items › #5 › support › high | “Bus” = autobús, bus. | “Bus” = autobús, bus. |
| items › #5 › support › medium | School is over. How do you go home? | Terminó la escuela. ¿Cómo te vas a casa? |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | the gym | the gym |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | the playground | the playground |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | the bus | the bus |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | The gym is inside the school. School is over, so you get on the bus. | El gimnasio está dentro de la escuela. Ya terminó la escuela, así que te subes al autobús. |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › hint | How do you go home? | ¿Cómo te vas a casa? |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | The playground is for recess. Your teacher said “get on the bus.” | El patio de juegos es para el recreo. Tu maestra dijo “get on the bus”. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | Listen again for the name of the place. | Escucha otra vez el nombre del lugar. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | Yes! At the end of the day you get on the bus to go home. “Bus” is like “bus” in Spanish. | ¡Sí! Al final del día te subes al autobús para ir a casa. “Bus” se dice igual en español. |
| items › #6 › context | Your teacher says: “Go to the restroom, then come back to the classroom.” | Tu maestra dice: “Go to the restroom, then come back to the classroom.” |
| items › #6 › prompt | Where do you go FIRST? | ¿A dónde vas PRIMERO? |
| items › #6 › support › high | “Restroom” = baño. “Then” = luego. | “Restroom” = baño. “Then” = luego. |
| items › #6 › support › medium | Two places! Which one comes first? | ¡Dos lugares! ¿Cuál va primero? |
| items › #6 › choices › #1 › label | the classroom | the classroom |
| items › #6 › choices › #2 › label | the restroom | the restroom |
| items › #6 › choices › #3 › label | the playground | the playground |
| items › #6 › feedback › a › why | You come back to the classroom second, after the restroom. “Then” tells you what comes next. | Al salón regresas después, en segundo lugar. “Then” (luego) te dice qué viene después. |
| items › #6 › feedback › a › hint | Listen for the place before “then.” | Escucha el lugar antes de “then”. |
| items › #6 › feedback › b › why | Yes! First the restroom, then back to the classroom. “Then” means next. | ¡Sí! Primero el baño (restroom) y luego regresas al salón. “Then” significa luego. |
| items › #6 › feedback › c › why | Your teacher did not say playground. First you go to the restroom. | Tu maestra no dijo playground. Primero vas al baño (restroom). |
| items › #6 › feedback › c › hint | Listen again for the name of the place. | Escucha otra vez el nombre del lugar. |

### Hello! Can You Help Me?  `nav-hello-k-2-013` — 77 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Hello! Can You Help Me? | ¡Hola! ¿Me ayudas? |
| studentGoal | Say hello, tell your name, and ask for what you need in English. | Saluda, di tu nombre y pide lo que necesitas en inglés. |
| studentDirections | Listen to the story. What do you say? Choose, then select Check. Then say it out loud! | Escucha la situación. ¿Qué dices? Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. ¡Después dilo en voz alta! |
| teacherDirections | Read each situation and all three choices aloud — K–2 students should not have to read them. After each item, have the class say the correct sentence together, then practice it with a partner using their own name. More support shows the Spanish meaning. These are the first social phrases a newcomer needs. | Lee en voz alta cada situación y las tres opciones; los estudiantes de K–2 no tienen que leerlas. Después de cada pregunta, pide a la clase que diga junta la oración correcta y luego la practique en parejas con su propio nombre. Más apoyo muestra el significado en español. Son las primeras frases sociales que necesita un recién llegado. |
| languageFunction | Greeting, introducing yourself, and asking for what you need. | Saludar, presentarse y pedir lo que se necesita. |
| items › #1 › context | It is your first day. A girl says: “Hi! I'm Lena. What's your name?” | Es tu primer día. Una niña dice: “Hi! I'm Lena. What's your name?” |
| items › #1 › prompt | What do you say? | ¿Qué dices? |
| items › #1 › support › high | “My name is…” = Me llamo… “Nice to meet you” = Mucho gusto. | “My name is…” = Me llamo… “Nice to meet you” = Mucho gusto. |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Start with “Hi! My name is…” | Empieza con “Hi! My name is…” |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | “I am six.” | “I am six.” |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | “Hi! My name is ___. Nice to meet you!” | “Hi! My name is ___. Nice to meet you!” |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | “Goodbye!” | “Goodbye!” |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | That tells your age, but she asked your name. | Eso dice tu edad, pero ella preguntó tu nombre. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › hint | She asked “What's your name?” | Ella preguntó “What's your name?” (¿Cómo te llamas?). |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | Yes! Say hi, tell your name, and say “Nice to meet you.” That is a friendly answer. | ¡Sí! Saluda, di tu nombre y di “Nice to meet you” (Mucho gusto). Es una respuesta amable. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | “Goodbye” is for leaving. When you meet someone, say hi and tell your name. | “Goodbye” (adiós) es para irte. Cuando conoces a alguien, saluda y di tu nombre. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › hint | Is she saying hello or goodbye? | ¿Ella dice hola o adiós? |
| items › #2 › context | It is morning. Your teacher, Ms. Diaz, is at the classroom door. | Es de mañana. Tu maestra, Ms. Diaz, está en la puerta del salón. |
| items › #2 › prompt | What do you say? | ¿Qué dices? |
| items › #2 › support › high | “Good morning” = Buenos días. | “Good morning” = Buenos días. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | It is the morning. Say hello. | Es de mañana. Saluda. |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | “Give me paper.” | “Give me paper.” |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | “Good night!” | “Good night!” |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | “Good morning, Ms. Diaz!” | “Good morning, Ms. Diaz!” |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | That sounds bossy, and it is not a hello. In the morning, say “Good morning!” | Eso suena mandón y no es un saludo. En la mañana, di “Good morning!” |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › hint | What do you say when you see someone in the morning? | ¿Qué dices cuando ves a alguien en la mañana? |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | “Good night” is for bedtime. In the morning, say “Good morning!” | “Good night” es para la hora de dormir. En la mañana, di “Good morning!” |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Is it morning or night? | ¿Es de mañana o de noche? |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | Yes! “Good morning” plus your teacher's name is a polite hello. | ¡Sí! “Good morning” (buenos días) con el nombre de tu maestra es un saludo cortés. |
| items › #3 › context | You need a crayon. Your friend has a box of crayons. | Necesitas un crayón. Tu amigo tiene una caja de crayones. |
| items › #3 › prompt | What do you say? | ¿Qué dices? |
| items › #3 › support › high | “Can I use…, please?” = ¿Puedo usar…, por favor? | “Can I use…, please?” = ¿Puedo usar…, por favor? |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Ask for it and say please. | Pídelo y di please. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | “Can I use a crayon, please?” | “Can I use a crayon, please?” |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | “Crayon!” | “Crayon!” |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | “I like red.” | “I like red.” |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | Yes! You ask clearly and say “please.” Your friend knows what you need. | ¡Sí! Pides con claridad y dices “please” (por favor). Tu amigo sabe qué necesitas. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | One word sounds bossy. Ask with “Can I…, please?” | Una sola palabra suena mandona. Pide con “Can I…, please?” |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | Which one is friendly and says please? | ¿Cuál es amable y dice please? |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | That tells what you like, but it does not ask for a crayon. | Eso dice lo que te gusta, pero no pide un crayón. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › hint | Did you ask for something? | ¿Pediste algo? |
| items › #4 › context | You are thirsty. You want a drink of water. | Tienes sed. Quieres tomar agua. |
| items › #4 › prompt | What do you say to your teacher? | ¿Qué le dices a tu maestra? |
| items › #4 › support › high | “May I get a drink of water, please?” = ¿Puedo ir a tomar agua, por favor? | “May I get a drink of water, please?” = ¿Puedo ir a tomar agua, por favor? |
| items › #4 › support › medium | Start with “May I…” | Empieza con “May I…” |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | “Water.” | “Water.” |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | “May I get a drink of water, please?” | “May I get a drink of water, please?” |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | “I am hot.” | “I am hot.” |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | One word is not a full question. Ask with “May I…, please?” | Una palabra no es una pregunta completa. Pregunta con “May I…, please?” |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › hint | Which one is friendly and says please? | ¿Cuál es amable y dice please? |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | Yes! “May I…, please?” is a polite way to ask a teacher. | ¡Sí! “May I…, please?” es una manera cortés de pedirle algo a la maestra. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | That tells how you feel, but it does not ask to get water. | Eso dice cómo te sientes, pero no pide ir por agua. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › hint | Did you ask for water? | ¿Pediste agua? |
| items › #5 › context | Some kids are building with blocks. You want to play with them. | Unos niños están construyendo con bloques. Quieres jugar con ellos. |
| items › #5 › prompt | What do you say? | ¿Qué dices? |
| items › #5 › support › high | “Can I play too?” = ¿Puedo jugar también? | “Can I play too?” = ¿Puedo jugar también? |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Ask to join. | Pide unirte. |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | “Go away.” | “Go away.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | “Blocks.” | “Blocks.” |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | “Can I play too?” | “Can I play too?” |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | “Go away” tells people to leave. You want to join them! | “Go away” (vete) les dice que se vayan. ¡Tú quieres jugar con ellos! |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › hint | Do you want to join or not? | ¿Quieres unirte o no? |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | One word does not ask a question. Ask “Can I play too?” | Una palabra no hace una pregunta. Pregunta “Can I play too?” |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | Ask a question to join. | Haz una pregunta para unirte. |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | Yes! “Can I play too?” is a friendly way to ask to join. | ¡Sí! “Can I play too?” (¿Puedo jugar también?) es una manera amable de pedir unirte. |
| items › #6 › context | You do not know what to do on your paper. You raise your hand. | No sabes qué hacer en tu hoja. Levantas la mano. |
| items › #6 › prompt | What do you say? | ¿Qué dices? |
| items › #6 › support › high | “Can you help me, please?” = ¿Me ayuda, por favor? | “Can you help me, please?” = ¿Me ayuda, por favor? |
| items › #6 › support › medium | Ask for help. | Pide ayuda. |
| items › #6 › choices › #1 › label | “I'm done.” | “I'm done.” |
| items › #6 › choices › #2 › label | “Can you help me, please?” | “Can you help me, please?” |
| items › #6 › choices › #3 › label | “No.” | “No.” |
| items › #6 › feedback › a › why | “I'm done” means you finished. You need help first. | “I'm done” significa que terminaste. Primero necesitas ayuda. |
| items › #6 › feedback › a › hint | Did you finish, or do you need help? | ¿Terminaste o necesitas ayuda? |
| items › #6 › feedback › b › why | Yes! Raise your hand and say “Can you help me, please?” Your teacher wants to help. | ¡Sí! Levanta la mano y di “Can you help me, please?” (¿Me ayuda, por favor?). Tu maestra quiere ayudarte. |
| items › #6 › feedback › c › why | “No” does not tell your teacher what you need. Ask for help. | “No” no le dice a tu maestra lo que necesitas. Pide ayuda. |
| items › #6 › feedback › c › hint | Ask for help with a question. | Pide ayuda con una pregunta. |

### Principal or Friend?  `nav-register-k-2-014` — 41 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Principal or Friend? | ¿A la directora o a un amigo? |
| studentGoal | Decide if words are better for a grown-up at school or for a friend. | Decide si unas palabras son mejores para un adulto de la escuela o para un amigo. |
| studentDirections | Listen to each sentence. Who would you say it to — a grown-up at school, or a friend? Put it in a box, then select Check. | Escucha cada oración. ¿A quién se la dirías: a un adulto de la escuela o a un amigo? Ponla en una caja y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Read each sentence aloud twice — once in a formal voice, once in a playground voice. The idea for young newcomers: both ways are fine, but we use titles (Mr., Ms.), “Excuse me,” and “Yes, ma'am” with grown-ups, and short, playful words with friends. After sorting, have students say a grown-up sentence to you and a friend sentence to a partner. More support shows the Spanish meaning. | Lee cada oración en voz alta dos veces: una con voz formal y otra con voz de recreo. La idea para los recién llegados pequeños: las dos maneras están bien, pero con los adultos usamos títulos (Mr., Ms.), “Excuse me” y “Yes, ma'am”, y con los amigos palabras cortas y juguetonas. Después de clasificar, pide a los estudiantes que te digan una oración para adultos y le digan a un compañero una oración para amigos. Más apoyo muestra el significado en español. |
| languageFunction | Choosing formal or informal words for the listener (register). | Elegir palabras formales o informales según quién escucha (registro). |
| categories › #1 › label | Say it to a grown-up (principal, teacher) | Para un adulto (directora, maestra) |
| categories › #1 › hint | Polite words, Mr. or Ms., “Excuse me,” “Yes, ma'am.” | Palabras corteses, Mr. o Ms., “Excuse me”, “Yes, ma'am”. |
| categories › #2 › label | Say it to a friend | Para un amigo |
| categories › #2 › hint | Short, fun, casual words. | Palabras cortas, divertidas e informales. |
| items › #1 › prompt | Good morning, Mr. Lee. | Good morning, Mr. Lee. |
| items › #1 › support › high | Buenos días, señor Lee. | Buenos días, señor Lee. |
| items › #1 › feedback › grownup › why | “Good morning” plus “Mr.” and a last name is how we greet a grown-up, like the principal. | “Good morning” con “Mr.” y el apellido es como saludamos a un adulto, como el director. |
| items › #1 › feedback › grownup › hint | Look for Mr. or Ms. before a name. | Busca Mr. o Ms. antes de un nombre. |
| items › #2 › prompt | Hey! Want to play? | Hey! Want to play? |
| items › #2 › support › high | ¡Oye! ¿Quieres jugar? | ¡Oye! ¿Quieres jugar? |
| items › #2 › feedback › friend › why | “Hey!” and a short question are playful words for a friend at recess. | “Hey!” y una pregunta corta son palabras juguetonas para un amigo en el recreo. |
| items › #2 › feedback › friend › hint | Is this how you talk at recess? | ¿Así hablas en el recreo? |
| items › #3 › prompt | Cool! My turn! | Cool! My turn! |
| items › #3 › support › high | ¡Genial! ¡Me toca! | ¡Genial! ¡Me toca! |
| items › #3 › feedback › friend › why | “Cool!” and “My turn!” are fun, short words for a friend during a game. | “Cool!” y “My turn!” son palabras cortas y divertidas para un amigo durante un juego. |
| items › #3 › feedback › friend › hint | Would you say “Cool!” to the principal? | ¿Le dirías “Cool!” a la directora? |
| items › #4 › prompt | Excuse me, may I ask a question? | Excuse me, may I ask a question? |
| items › #4 › support › high | Disculpe, ¿puedo hacer una pregunta? | Disculpe, ¿puedo hacer una pregunta? |
| items › #4 › feedback › grownup › why | “Excuse me” and “may I” are polite words we use with a teacher or the principal. | “Excuse me” y “may I” son palabras corteses que usamos con la maestra o la directora. |
| items › #4 › feedback › grownup › hint | Polite words like “Excuse me” are for grown-ups. | Las palabras corteses como “Excuse me” son para los adultos. |
| items › #5 › prompt | See ya! | See ya! |
| items › #5 › support › high | ¡Nos vemos! | ¡Nos vemos! |
| items › #5 › feedback › friend › why | “See ya!” is a quick, casual goodbye for a friend. With a grown-up, say “Goodbye, Ms. Ruiz.” | “See ya!” es una despedida rápida e informal para un amigo. Con un adulto, di “Goodbye, Ms. Ruiz.” |
| items › #5 › feedback › friend › hint | Is this a short, casual goodbye? | ¿Es una despedida corta e informal? |
| items › #6 › prompt | Thank you very much, Ms. Ruiz. | Thank you very much, Ms. Ruiz. |
| items › #6 › support › high | Muchas gracias, señora Ruiz. | Muchas gracias, señora Ruiz. |
| items › #6 › feedback › grownup › why | “Thank you very much” plus “Ms.” and a last name is a polite way to thank a grown-up. | “Thank you very much” con “Ms.” y el apellido es una manera cortés de darle las gracias a un adulto. |
| items › #6 › feedback › grownup › hint | Look for Ms. before the name. | Busca Ms. antes del nombre. |
| items › #7 › prompt | Yeah, sure! | Yeah, sure! |
| items › #7 › support › high | ¡Sí, claro! | ¡Sí, claro! |
| items › #7 › feedback › friend › why | “Yeah” is the casual way to say yes. With a grown-up, say “Yes” or “Yes, ma'am.” | “Yeah” es la forma informal de decir sí. Con un adulto, di “Yes” o “Yes, ma'am.” |
| items › #7 › feedback › friend › hint | Is “yeah” casual or formal? | ¿“Yeah” es informal o formal? |
| items › #8 › prompt | Yes, ma'am. | Yes, ma'am. |
| items › #8 › support › high | Sí, señora. | Sí, señora. |
| items › #8 › feedback › grownup › why | “Yes, ma'am” is a respectful answer to a woman who is a grown-up, like a teacher. Many Texas schools use it. | “Yes, ma'am” es una respuesta respetuosa para una mujer adulta, como una maestra. Se usa mucho en las escuelas de Texas. |
| items › #8 › feedback › grownup › hint | “Ma'am” is a respectful word. | “Ma'am” es una palabra de respeto. |

### What Did the Nurse Say?  `nav-nurse-k-2-015` — 65 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | What Did the Nurse Say? | ¿Qué dijo la enfermera? |
| studentGoal | Listen to the school nurse and show you understood by choosing what she said. | Escucha a la enfermera de la escuela y demuestra que entendiste eligiendo lo que dijo. |
| studentDirections | Listen to the nurse. What did she say? Choose, then select Check. Tell a partner what the nurse said. | Escucha a la enfermera. ¿Qué dijo? Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. Cuéntale a un compañero lo que dijo la enfermera. |
| teacherDirections | Read the nurse's words aloud in a calm voice; students listen, then choose the retelling. After each item, have a student retell it in English with the frame “The nurse said…”. The last item practices asking the nurse to repeat. A good moment to walk students to the nurse's office and introduce the nurse. | Lee en voz alta y con calma lo que dice la enfermera; los estudiantes escuchan y eligen lo que significa. Después de cada pregunta, pide a un estudiante que lo vuelva a contar en inglés con el marco “The nurse said…”. La última pregunta practica cómo pedirle a la enfermera que repita. Es un buen momento para llevar a los estudiantes a la enfermería y presentarles a la enfermera. |
| languageFunction | Retelling spoken information and asking for clarification. | Volver a contar información hablada y pedir aclaración. |
| items › #1 › context | The nurse says: “Lie down on the bed and rest for ten minutes.” | La enfermera dice: “Lie down on the bed and rest for ten minutes.” |
| items › #1 › prompt | What did the nurse say? | ¿Qué dijo la enfermera? |
| items › #1 › support › high | “Lie down” = acuéstate. “Rest” = descansa. “Ten minutes” = diez minutos. | “Lie down” = acuéstate. “Rest” = descansa. “Ten minutes” = diez minutos. |
| items › #1 › support › medium | What do you do, and for how long? | ¿Qué haces y por cuánto tiempo? |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | Rest on the bed for ten minutes. | Descansa en la cama diez minutos. |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | Go out to recess. | Sal al recreo. |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | Go home now. | Vete a tu casa ahora. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | Yes! “Rest” means lie down and be still, for ten minutes. | ¡Sí! “Rest” significa acostarte y quedarte tranquilo, por diez minutos. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | The nurse said to rest, not to play outside. | La enfermera dijo que descanses, no que juegues afuera. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › hint | Listen again to what the nurse said. | Escucha otra vez lo que dijo la enfermera. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | The nurse did not say go home. She said rest for ten minutes. | La enfermera no dijo que te vayas a casa. Dijo que descanses diez minutos. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › hint | What did she say to do on the bed? | ¿Qué dijo que hagas en la cama? |
| items › #2 › context | The nurse says: “Drink some water. Then go back to class.” | La enfermera dice: “Drink some water. Then go back to class.” |
| items › #2 › prompt | What did the nurse say? | ¿Qué dijo la enfermera? |
| items › #2 › support › high | “Drink” = toma. “Water” = agua. “Go back to class” = regresa a clase. | “Drink” = toma. “Water” = agua. “Go back to class” = regresa a clase. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | There are two things. What are they? | Son dos cosas. ¿Cuáles son? |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | Stay with the nurse all day. | Quédate con la enfermera todo el día. |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | Eat your lunch. | Come tu almuerzo. |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | Drink some water, then go back to class. | Toma un poco de agua y luego regresa a clase. |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | The nurse said to go back to class after your water. | La enfermera dijo que regreses a clase después de tomar agua. |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › hint | Listen for “then.” | Escucha “then” (luego). |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | The nurse said “drink some water,” not eat lunch. | La enfermera dijo “drink some water” (toma agua), no que comas. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Listen again to what the nurse said. | Escucha otra vez lo que dijo la enfermera. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | Yes! First drink water, then go back to class. You remembered both parts. | ¡Sí! Primero tomas agua y luego regresas a clase. Recordaste las dos partes. |
| items › #3 › context | The nurse says: “I will call your family. They will come and take you home.” | La enfermera dice: “I will call your family. They will come and take you home.” |
| items › #3 › prompt | What did the nurse say? | ¿Qué dijo la enfermera? |
| items › #3 › support › high | “I will call your family” = Voy a llamar a tu familia. “Take you home” = llevarte a casa. | “I will call your family” = Voy a llamar a tu familia. “Take you home” = llevarte a casa. |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Who will she call? | ¿A quién va a llamar? |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | You will ride the bus home. | Te irás a casa en el autobús. |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | She will call your family, and they will take you home. | Llamará a tu familia y te llevarán a casa. |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | Your teacher will call your friend. | Tu maestra llamará a tu amigo. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | The nurse did not say bus. Your family will come to get you. | La enfermera no dijo autobús. Tu familia vendrá por ti. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › hint | Who is coming? | ¿Quién va a venir? |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | Yes! The nurse will call your family. Your family will come and take you home. | ¡Sí! La enfermera llamará a tu familia. Tu familia vendrá y te llevará a casa. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | The NURSE will call your FAMILY, not your teacher calling a friend. | La ENFERMERA llamará a tu FAMILIA, no tu maestra a un amigo. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › hint | Listen again to what the nurse said. | Escucha otra vez lo que dijo la enfermera. |
| items › #4 › context | You fell at recess. The nurse says: “Hold this ice pack on your knee.” | Te caíste en el recreo. La enfermera dice: “Hold this ice pack on your knee.” |
| items › #4 › prompt | What did the nurse say? | ¿Qué dijo la enfermera? |
| items › #4 › support › high | “Ice pack” = bolsa de hielo. “Knee” = rodilla. | “Ice pack” = bolsa de hielo. “Knee” = rodilla. |
| items › #4 › support › medium | What does she give you? Where does it go? | ¿Qué te da? ¿Dónde va? |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | Put a bandage on your arm. | Ponte una curita en el brazo. |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | Take off your shoes. | Quítate los zapatos. |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | Hold the ice pack on your knee. | Sostén la bolsa de hielo en tu rodilla. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | The nurse said ICE on your KNEE, not a bandage on your arm. | La enfermera dijo HIELO en la RODILLA, no una curita en el brazo. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › hint | Which body part did she say? | ¿Qué parte del cuerpo dijo? |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | The nurse did not talk about shoes. She said to hold the ice pack on your knee. | La enfermera no habló de zapatos. Dijo que sostengas la bolsa de hielo en tu rodilla. |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › hint | Listen again to what the nurse said. | Escucha otra vez lo que dijo la enfermera. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | Yes! An ice pack is cold. You hold it on your knee to help it feel better. | ¡Sí! La bolsa de hielo (ice pack) está fría. La sostienes en la rodilla para que se sienta mejor. |
| items › #5 › context | The nurse talks fast. You did not understand what she said. | La enfermera habla rápido. No entendiste lo que dijo. |
| items › #5 › prompt | What can you say to the nurse? | ¿Qué le puedes decir a la enfermera? |
| items › #5 › support › high | “Can you say it again, please?” = ¿Me lo puede repetir, por favor? | “Can you say it again, please?” = ¿Me lo puede repetir, por favor? |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Ask her to say it again. | Pídele que lo repita. |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | “Can you say it again, please?” | “Can you say it again, please?” |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | “OK.” (but you did not understand) | “OK.” (pero no entendiste) |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | “Bye!” | “Bye!” |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | Yes! When you do not understand, it is OK to ask the nurse to say it again. | ¡Sí! Cuando no entiendes, está bien pedirle a la enfermera que lo repita. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | Saying OK when you did not understand can be a problem. Ask her to say it again. | Decir OK cuando no entendiste puede ser un problema. Pídele que lo repita. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | Is it OK to ask again? | ¿Está bien volver a preguntar? |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | Leaving does not help you understand. Ask “Can you say it again, please?” | Irte no te ayuda a entender. Pregunta “Can you say it again, please?” |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › hint | What can you ask so she repeats it? | ¿Qué puedes preguntar para que lo repita? |

### Classroom Routines  `nav-routines-k-2-016` — 77 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Classroom Routines | Las rutinas del salón |
| studentGoal | Understand the words for everyday class routines and do the right thing. | Entiende las palabras de las rutinas diarias de la clase y haz lo correcto. |
| studentDirections | Listen to your teacher. What do you do? Choose, then select Check. Then show it with your body! | Escucha a tu maestra. ¿Qué haces? Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. ¡Después muéstralo con tu cuerpo! |
| teacherDirections | These are the routine phrases young newcomers hear every day — arriving, lunch, the bathroom pass, clean-up, and the attention signal. Read each one aloud and act it out the first time (Total Physical Response); then say it again and let students do it. Use your own class's routine words if they differ (for example, your attention signal). More support shows key words in Spanish. | Estas son las frases de rutina que los recién llegados pequeños oyen todos los días: la llegada, el almuerzo, el pase para el baño, la limpieza y la señal de atención. Lee cada una en voz alta y represéntala la primera vez (Respuesta Física Total); luego repítela y deja que los estudiantes la hagan. Usa las palabras de rutina de tu propia clase si son diferentes (por ejemplo, tu señal de atención). Más apoyo muestra palabras clave en español. |
| languageFunction | Understanding and acting on routine classroom language. | Comprender y seguir el lenguaje de las rutinas del salón. |
| items › #1 › context | It is the morning. Your teacher says: “Hang up your backpack and sit on the carpet.” | Es de mañana. Tu maestra dice: “Hang up your backpack and sit on the carpet.” |
| items › #1 › prompt | What do you do? | ¿Qué haces? |
| items › #1 › support › high | “Hang up” = cuelga. “Backpack” = mochila. “Carpet” = alfombra. | “Hang up” = cuelga. “Backpack” = mochila. “Carpet” = alfombra. |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Two steps: the backpack, then you. | Dos pasos: la mochila y luego tú. |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | Keep your backpack on and go to recess. | Déjate la mochila puesta y vete al recreo. |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | Hang your backpack on the hook, then sit on the carpet. | Cuelga tu mochila en el gancho y luego siéntate en la alfombra. |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | Sit at your desk and open your backpack. | Siéntate en tu escritorio y abre tu mochila. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | “Hang up” means put it on the hook. Then you sit on the carpet, not go to recess. | “Hang up” significa colgarla en el gancho. Luego te sientas en la alfombra, no vas al recreo. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › hint | What does “hang up” mean? | ¿Qué significa “hang up”? |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | Yes! First hang up your backpack, then sit on the carpet. | ¡Sí! Primero cuelgas la mochila y luego te sientas en la alfombra. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | Your teacher said the carpet, and the backpack goes on the hook. | Tu maestra dijo la alfombra, y la mochila va en el gancho. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › hint | Listen again to your teacher's words. | Escucha otra vez las palabras de tu maestra. |
| items › #2 › context | Your teacher says: “Lunch time! Get your lunch box and line up.” | Tu maestra dice: “Lunch time! Get your lunch box and line up.” |
| items › #2 › prompt | What do you do? | ¿Qué haces? |
| items › #2 › support › high | “Lunch box” = lonchera. “Line up” = hagan fila. | “Lunch box” = lonchera. “Line up” = hagan fila. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | Take something, then make a line. | Toma algo y luego haz fila. |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | Get your lunch box and stand in line. | Toma tu lonchera y párate en la fila. |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | Put your lunch box in your backpack. | Guarda tu lonchera en tu mochila. |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | Eat your lunch at your desk. | Come tu almuerzo en tu escritorio. |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | Yes! Get your lunch box, then line up to walk to the cafeteria. | ¡Sí! Toma tu lonchera y luego haz fila para caminar a la cafetería. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | “Get your lunch box” means take it out to bring it with you. Then line up. | “Get your lunch box” significa tomarla para llevarla contigo. Luego haz fila. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Does “get” mean take it or put it away? | ¿“Get” significa tomarla o guardarla? |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | Your teacher said “line up.” You eat in the cafeteria. | Tu maestra dijo “line up” (hagan fila). Se come en la cafetería. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › hint | Listen again to your teacher's words. | Escucha otra vez las palabras de tu maestra. |
| items › #3 › context | You need the restroom. Your teacher says: “Take the bathroom pass and come right back.” | Necesitas ir al baño. Tu maestra dice: “Take the bathroom pass and come right back.” |
| items › #3 › prompt | What do you do? | ¿Qué haces? |
| items › #3 › support › high | “Bathroom pass” = pase para el baño. “Come right back” = regresa rápido. | “Bathroom pass” = pase para el baño. “Come right back” = regresa rápido. |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Take something with you, then come back. | Lleva algo contigo y luego regresa. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | Go to the restroom and stay outside to play. | Ve al baño y quédate afuera a jugar. |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | Leave the pass on your desk and run out. | Deja el pase en tu escritorio y sal corriendo. |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | Take the bathroom pass, go, and come back fast. | Toma el pase del baño, ve y regresa rápido. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | “Come right back” means come back to class fast after the restroom. | “Come right back” significa regresar rápido a clase después del baño. |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › hint | What does “come right back” mean? | ¿Qué significa “come right back”? |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › why | Your teacher said to TAKE the pass with you. The pass shows you have permission. | Tu maestra dijo que TOMES el pase. El pase muestra que tienes permiso. |
| items › #3 › feedback › b › hint | Listen again to your teacher's words. | Escucha otra vez las palabras de tu maestra. |
| items › #3 › feedback › c › why | Yes! Take the pass with you, use the restroom, and come right back to class. | ¡Sí! Llevas el pase, usas el baño y regresas rápido a clase. |
| items › #4 › context | Your teacher says: “Clean up! Put the crayons in the box.” | Tu maestra dice: “Clean up! Put the crayons in the box.” |
| items › #4 › prompt | What do you do? | ¿Qué haces? |
| items › #4 › support › high | “Clean up” = recojan. “Put … in the box” = pongan … en la caja. | “Clean up” = recojan. “Put … in the box” = pongan … en la caja. |
| items › #4 › support › medium | Where do the crayons go? | ¿Dónde van los crayones? |
| items › #4 › choices › #1 › label | Keep drawing with the crayons. | Sigue dibujando con los crayones. |
| items › #4 › choices › #2 › label | Put the crayons back in the box. | Guarda los crayones en la caja. |
| items › #4 › choices › #3 › label | Give the crayons to a friend. | Dale los crayones a un amigo. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › why | “Clean up” means stop and put things away. | “Clean up” significa parar y guardar las cosas. |
| items › #4 › feedback › a › hint | What does “clean up” mean? | ¿Qué significa “clean up”? |
| items › #4 › feedback › b › why | Yes! “Clean up” means put things away. The crayons go in the box. | ¡Sí! “Clean up” significa guardar las cosas. Los crayones van en la caja. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › why | Your teacher said “in the box,” not to a friend. | Tu maestra dijo “in the box” (en la caja), no a un amigo. |
| items › #4 › feedback › c › hint | Listen again to your teacher's words. | Escucha otra vez las palabras de tu maestra. |
| items › #5 › context | Your class is busy and loud. Your teacher says: “Class, class! Eyes on me.” | Tu clase está ocupada y hay ruido. Tu maestra dice: “Class, class! Eyes on me.” |
| items › #5 › prompt | What do you do? | ¿Qué haces? |
| items › #5 › support › high | “Eyes on me” = mírenme. | “Eyes on me” = mírenme. |
| items › #5 › support › medium | Where do you look? | ¿A dónde miras? |
| items › #5 › choices › #1 › label | Keep talking to your friend. | Sigue hablando con tu amigo. |
| items › #5 › choices › #2 › label | Close your eyes. | Cierra los ojos. |
| items › #5 › choices › #3 › label | Stop, be quiet, and look at your teacher. | Detente, guarda silencio y mira a tu maestra. |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › why | “Eyes on me” means stop talking and look at the teacher. | “Eyes on me” significa dejar de hablar y mirar a la maestra. |
| items › #5 › feedback › a › hint | What does “eyes on me” mean? | ¿Qué significa “eyes on me”? |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › why | “Eyes on me” does not mean close your eyes. It means look at the teacher. | “Eyes on me” no significa cerrar los ojos. Significa mirar a la maestra. |
| items › #5 › feedback › b › hint | Where should your eyes look? | ¿A dónde deben mirar tus ojos? |
| items › #5 › feedback › c › why | Yes! “Eyes on me” means stop, get quiet, and look at the teacher so you can listen. | ¡Sí! “Eyes on me” significa parar, guardar silencio y mirar a la maestra para poder escuchar. |
| items › #6 › context | You finished your work and you stand up. Your teacher says: “Push in your chair, please.” | Terminaste tu trabajo y te levantas. Tu maestra dice: “Push in your chair, please.” |
| items › #6 › prompt | What do you do? | ¿Qué haces? |
| items › #6 › support › high | “Push in your chair” = mete tu silla. | “Push in your chair” = mete tu silla. |
| items › #6 › support › medium | Where does the chair go? | ¿Dónde va la silla? |
| items › #6 › choices › #1 › label | Push your chair under your desk. | Mete tu silla debajo de tu escritorio. |
| items › #6 › choices › #2 › label | Stand on your chair. | Párate en tu silla. |
| items › #6 › choices › #3 › label | Take your chair outside. | Saca tu silla afuera. |
| items › #6 › feedback › a › why | Yes! “Push in your chair” means slide it under the desk so no one trips. | ¡Sí! “Push in your chair” significa meter la silla debajo del escritorio para que nadie se tropiece. |
| items › #6 › feedback › b › why | Standing on a chair is not safe. “Push in” means slide it under the desk. | Pararte en la silla no es seguro. “Push in” significa meterla debajo del escritorio. |
| items › #6 › feedback › b › hint | What does “push in” mean? | ¿Qué significa “push in”? |
| items › #6 › feedback › c › why | The chair stays in the classroom. Push it in, under your desk. | La silla se queda en el salón. Métela debajo de tu escritorio. |
| items › #6 › feedback › c › hint | Listen again to your teacher's words. | Escucha otra vez las palabras de tu maestra. |

### Reading the Lunch Menu  `nav-menu-3-5-017` — 65 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | Reading the Lunch Menu | Leer el menú del almuerzo |
| studentGoal | Read a school lunch menu in English and find the information you need. | Lee un menú escolar del almuerzo en inglés y encuentra la información que necesitas. |
| studentDirections | Read the lunch menu. Answer each question. Choose, then select Check. | Lee el menú del almuerzo. Responde cada pregunta. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Project the menu (it repeats in every item) or hand out your school's real menu. Model how to scan: find the day first, then read across. Point out the cognates (vegetarian/vegetariano, salad/ensalada, banana/banana) and that the menu is organized by day. Turn on More support for key words in Spanish. | Proyecta el menú (se repite en cada pregunta) o reparte el menú real de tu escuela. Modela cómo buscar: primero encuentra el día y luego lee a lo largo. Señala los cognados (vegetarian/vegetariano, salad/ensalada, banana/banana) y que el menú está organizado por día. Activa Más apoyo para ver palabras clave en español. |
| languageFunction | Locating information in a school notice (lunch menu). | Localizar información en un aviso escolar (menú del almuerzo). |
| items › #1 › context | LUNCH MENU — Week of March 2. Monday: chicken tacos, corn, apple slices. Tuesday: cheese pizza, salad, orange. Wednesday: spaghetti and meatballs, green beans. Thursday: turkey sandwich, carrot sticks, banana. Friday: fish sticks, sweet potato fries, pear. Every day: milk (white or chocolate). Vegetarian choice every day: bean and cheese burrito. | El menú dice: “LUNCH MENU — Week of March 2. Monday: chicken tacos, corn, apple slices. Tuesday: cheese pizza, salad, orange. Wednesday: spaghetti and meatballs, green beans. Thursday: turkey sandwich, carrot sticks, banana. Friday: fish sticks, sweet potato fries, pear. Every day: milk (white or chocolate). Vegetarian choice every day: bean and cheese burrito.” |
| items › #1 › prompt | Which day is pizza for lunch? | ¿Qué día hay pizza para el almuerzo? |
| items › #1 › support › high | Monday = lunes, Tuesday = martes, Friday = viernes. | Monday = lunes, Tuesday = martes, Friday = viernes. |
| items › #1 › support › medium | Find the word “pizza.” | Busca la palabra “pizza”. |
| items › #1 › choices › #1 › label | Monday | Monday (lunes) |
| items › #1 › choices › #2 › label | Tuesday | Tuesday (martes) |
| items › #1 › choices › #3 › label | Friday | Friday (viernes) |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › why | Monday is chicken tacos. Look for the word “pizza.” | El lunes hay tacos de pollo. Busca la palabra “pizza”. |
| items › #1 › feedback › a › hint | Find the line in the notice that answers the question. | Busca la línea del aviso que responde la pregunta. |
| items › #1 › feedback › b › why | Right. The menu says “Tuesday: cheese pizza.” | Correcto. El menú dice “Tuesday: cheese pizza”. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › why | Friday is fish sticks. Pizza is on Tuesday. | El viernes hay palitos de pescado. La pizza es el martes. |
| items › #1 › feedback › c › hint | Find “pizza,” then look at the day before it. | Busca “pizza” y mira el día que está antes. |
| items › #2 › context | LUNCH MENU — Week of March 2. Monday: chicken tacos, corn, apple slices. Tuesday: cheese pizza, salad, orange. Wednesday: spaghetti and meatballs, green beans. Thursday: turkey sandwich, carrot sticks, banana. Friday: fish sticks, sweet potato fries, pear. Every day: milk (white or chocolate). Vegetarian choice every day: bean and cheese burrito. | El menú dice: “LUNCH MENU — Week of March 2. Monday: chicken tacos, corn, apple slices. Tuesday: cheese pizza, salad, orange. Wednesday: spaghetti and meatballs, green beans. Thursday: turkey sandwich, carrot sticks, banana. Friday: fish sticks, sweet potato fries, pear. Every day: milk (white or chocolate). Vegetarian choice every day: bean and cheese burrito.” |
| items › #2 › prompt | What does “vegetarian” most likely mean here? | ¿Qué significa probablemente “vegetarian” aquí? |
| items › #2 › support › high | Cognate: vegetarian = vegetariano. Bean = frijol. Cheese = queso. | Cognado: vegetarian = vegetariano. Bean = frijol. Cheese = queso. |
| items › #2 › support › medium | What food is the vegetarian choice? | ¿Qué comida es la opción vegetariana? |
| items › #2 › choices › #1 › label | Food that is only vegetables, like salad | Comida que es solo verduras, como ensalada |
| items › #2 › choices › #2 › label | The biggest lunch | El almuerzo más grande |
| items › #2 › choices › #3 › label | Food with no meat | Comida sin carne |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › why | The vegetarian choice is a bean and cheese burrito — not only vegetables. Vegetarian means no meat. | La opción vegetariana es un burrito de frijoles y queso, no solo verduras. Vegetarian significa sin carne. |
| items › #2 › feedback › a › hint | Look at the food in the vegetarian choice. Does it have meat? | Mira la comida de la opción vegetariana. ¿Tiene carne? |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › why | Nothing on the menu says it is bigger. Look at what the burrito has in it. | Nada en el menú dice que es más grande. Mira qué tiene el burrito. |
| items › #2 › feedback › b › hint | Use the Spanish cognate to help. | Usa el cognado en español como ayuda. |
| items › #2 › feedback › c › why | Right. A bean and cheese burrito has no meat. “Vegetarian” is a cognate of “vegetariano.” | Correcto. Un burrito de frijoles y queso no tiene carne. “Vegetarian” es cognado de “vegetariano”. |
| items › #3 › context | LUNCH MENU — Week of March 2. Monday: chicken tacos, corn, apple slices. Tuesday: cheese pizza, salad, orange. Wednesday: spaghetti and meatballs, green beans. Thursday: turkey sandwich, carrot sticks, banana. Friday: fish sticks, sweet potato fries, pear. Every day: milk (white or chocolate). Vegetarian choice every day: bean and cheese burrito. | El menú dice: “LUNCH MENU — Week of March 2. Monday: chicken tacos, corn, apple slices. Tuesday: cheese pizza, salad, orange. Wednesday: spaghetti and meatballs, green beans. Thursday: turkey sandwich, carrot sticks, banana. Friday: fish sticks, sweet potato fries, pear. Every day: milk (white or chocolate). Vegetarian choice every day: bean and cheese burrito.” |
| items › #3 › prompt | You do not eat meat. What can you eat on Thursday? | No comes carne. ¿Qué puedes comer el jueves? |
| items › #3 › support › high | “Every day” = todos los días. Turkey = pavo. | “Every day” = todos los días. Turkey = pavo. |
| items › #3 › support › medium | Look at the last line of the menu. | Mira la última línea del menú. |
| items › #3 › choices › #1 › label | A bean and cheese burrito | Un burrito de frijoles y queso |
| items › #3 › choices › #2 › label | A turkey sandwich | Un sándwich de pavo |
| items › #3 › choices › #3 › label | Nothing — there is no lunch for you | Nada; no hay almuerzo para ti |
| items › #3 › feedback › a › why | Right. Thursday has a turkey sandwich (meat), but the vegetarian burrito is there every day. | Correcto. El jueves hay sándwich de pavo (carne), pero el burrito vegetariano está todos los días. |

### The Field Trip Note  `nav-fieldtrip-3-5-019` — 65 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | The Field Trip Note | La nota de la excursión |
| studentGoal | Read a field-trip note from school and find the important information. | Lee una nota de la escuela sobre una excursión y encuentra la información importante. |
| studentDirections | Read the note from the teacher. Answer each question. Choose, then select Check. | Lee la nota de la maestra. Responde cada pregunta. Elige y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Project the note (it repeats in every item). Teach the shape of a school letter: who it is to, the event, the dates, what to bring, what to sign. Two dates appear — the trip date and the return-by date — which is a common mix-up. Encourage students to read the note with their family at home; many families will want a home-language copy. | Proyecta la nota (se repite en cada pregunta). Enseña la estructura de una carta escolar: a quién va dirigida, el evento, las fechas, qué llevar y qué firmar. Aparecen dos fechas, la del viaje y la fecha límite para entregarla, y es fácil confundirlas. Anima a los estudiantes a leer la nota con su familia en casa; muchas familias querrán una copia en su idioma. |
| languageFunction | Locating key details (what, when, what to bring, what to do) in a school letter. | Localizar detalles clave (qué, cuándo, qué llevar, qué hacer) en una carta escolar. |

We will leave school at 9:00 a.m. and come back by 2:00 p.m. Please send a sack lunch, a water bottle, and comfortable shoes. The trip is free. ### School Signs: Do, Don't, Be Careful  `nav-signs-3-5-021` — 47 strings

| Field | English | Spanish |
|---|---|---|
| title | School Signs: Do, Don't, Be Careful | Letreros de la escuela: hazlo, no lo hagas, ten cuidado |
| studentGoal | Read school signs in English and tell if each one says to do something, not to do something, or to be careful. | Lee letreros de la escuela en inglés y decide si cada uno dice que hagas algo, que no lo hagas o que tengas cuidado. |
| studentDirections | Read each sign. Does it tell you to DO something, NOT to do something, or to BE CAREFUL? Place each sign, then select Check. | Lee cada letrero. ¿Te dice que HAGAS algo, que NO lo hagas o que TENGAS CUIDADO? Coloca cada letrero y luego selecciona Revisar. |
| teacherDirections | Teach the three signal words first: “No” and “Do not” mean don't; “Caution” and “Watch” warn you to be careful; a plain action verb (Walk, Wash, Keep) tells you what to do. After sorting, take a sign walk around your campus and sort the real signs you find. More support shows each sign in Spanish. | Enseña primero las tres palabras señal: “No” y “Do not” significan no lo hagas; “Caution” y “Watch” te advierten que tengas cuidado; un verbo de acción solo (Walk, Wash, Keep) te dice qué hacer. Después de clasificar, hagan un recorrido por el plantel y clasifiquen los letreros reales que encuentren. Más apoyo muestra cada letrero en español. |
| languageFunction | Understanding rules and warnings on school signs. | Comprender reglas y advertencias en los letreros de la escuela. |

| categories › #2 › hint | Look for “No” or “Do not.” | Busca “No” o “Do not”. |
| categories › #3 › label | Be careful | Ten cuidado |
| categories › #3 › hint | Look for a warning: “Caution” or “Watch.” | Busca una advertencia: “Caution” o “Watch”. |

| items › #1 › feedback › do › hint | Does it start with an action word? | ¿Empieza con una palabra de acción? |
| items › #2 › prompt | No running | No running |
| items › #2 › support › high | Prohibido correr. | Prohibido correr. |
| items › #2 › feedback › dont › why | “No” before an action means don't do it. Don't run. | “No” antes de una acción significa que no lo hagas. No corras. |
| items › #2 › feedback › dont › hint | Look at the first word. | Mira la primera palabra. |
